\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
, which is a quadratic form associated with the matrix
\tau(A)
.

For a quadratic form
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
, we say that
\tilde{M}
is
pure/homogeneous/
p
-non-degenerate
if the quadratic form
M:=\iota\circ p\tilde{M}\circ\tau
induced by
\tilde{M}
is pure/homogeneous/non-degenerate.
The
p
-rank
of
\tilde{M}
, denoted by
\text{rank}_{p}(\tilde{M})
, is defined to be the rank of
M
.

We say that
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
are
p
-linearly independent
if for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}/p
,
c_{1}h_{1}+\dots+c_{k}h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}

implies that
c_{1},\dots,c_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}
, or equivalently, if
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are linearly independent.

We can also lift the definition of set of zeros and Gowers sets to the
\mathbb{Z}/p
setting.

•

For a polynomial
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\mathbb{R})
, let
V_{p}(P)
denote the set of
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
such that
P(n+pm)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.

•

For
h_{1},\dots,h_{t}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, let
V_{p}(P)^{h_{1},\dots,h_{t}}
denote the set of
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
such that
P(n+pm),P(n+h_{1}+pm),\dots,P(n+h_{t}+pm)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.

•

For
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
s\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\Box_{p,s}(\Omega)
denote the set of
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}
such that
n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s}h_{s}\in\Omega+p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s}\in\{0,1\}
.
We say that
\Box_{p,s}(\Omega)
is the
s
-th
p
-Gowers set
of
\Omega
.

The following lemma is straightforward:

Lemma 7.2
.

For any
p
-periodic set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}
(recall the definition in Section
1.4
) and
K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have that

\frac{1}{p^{k}}|\Omega\cap[p]^{k}|=\frac{1}{(pK)^{k}}|\Omega\cap[pK]^{k}|.

7.2.
Lifting results from
\mathbb{F}_{p}
to
\mathbb{Z}/p

Using the connections between
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-valued polynomials and
\mathbb{Z}/p
-valued polynomials, one can easily extend many results in Section
6
to the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-setting.
The following is an example:

Corollary 7.3

(Lifting of Proposition
6.3
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\gg_{d,s}1
be a prime number,

P\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p|\mathbb{Z})
be a polynomial of degree at most
s
, and
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a quadratic form of
p
-rank at least 3. Then either
|V_{p}(M)\cap V_{p}(P)\cap[p]^{d}|\leq O_{d,s}(p^{d-2})
or
V_{p}(M)\subseteq V_{p}(P)
.

Moreover, if
V_{p}(M)\subseteq V_{p}(P)
, then
P=MP_{1}+P_{0}
for some

P_{0}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s
, and some integer coefficient polynomial
P_{1}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s-2
.

Proof.

Let
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form of rank at least 3 induced by
M
,
P^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the polynomial of degree at most
s
induced by
P
(whose existence is given by Lemma
A.1
(i)).
By Proposition
6.3
, either
|V(M^{\prime})\cap V(P^{\prime})|\leq O_{d,s}(p^{d-2})
or
V(M^{\prime})\subseteq V(P^{\prime})
. By the definition of lifting, the form case implies that
|V_{p}(M)\cap V_{p}(P)\cap[p]^{d}|=|V(M^{\prime})\cap V(P^{\prime})|\leq O_{d,s}(p^{d-2})
, and the later case implies that
V_{p}(M)\subseteq V_{p}(P)
.

We now assume that
V_{p}(M)\subseteq V_{p}(P)
. By the definition of lifting, we have that
V(M^{\prime})\subseteq V(P^{\prime})
. By Proposition
6.3
,
P^{\prime}=M^{\prime}Q^{\prime}
for some
Q^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
of degree at most
s-2
.
Let
Q\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p)
be a regular lifting of
Q^{\prime}
of degree at most
s-2
(whose existence is given by Lemma
A.1
(ii)). By Lemma
A.1
(iv) and (v),
P-pMQ
is a lifting of
P^{\prime}-M^{\prime}Q^{\prime}\equiv 0
.
Then by Lemma
A.1
(iii), we have that
P-pMQ
is integer valued and is of degree at most
s
.
By Lemma
2.8
, we may write
Q=pQ_{1}+Q_{2}
for some
\mathbb{Z}
-valued polynomial
Q_{1}
and some integer coefficient polynomial
Q_{2}
both having degrees at most
s-2
.
Then

P=MQ_{2}+((P-pMQ)+pMQ_{1})
with
(P-pMQ)+pMQ_{1}
being an integer valued polynomial of degree at most
s
. We are done.
∎

The approach in the proof of Corollary
7.3
can be adapted to lift many other results from the
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-setting to the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-setting. Below are the liftings of some results from Section
6
that will be used in the further.
We leave the proofs to the interested readers.

Corollary 7.4

(Lifting of Corollary
6.4
)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\gg_{d,k,s}1
be a prime number,

P\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p|\mathbb{Z})
be a polynomial of degree at most
s
,
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a non-degenerate quadratic form induced by some
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
with a basis
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
for some
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Denote

L_{i}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(h_{i}\tau(A))\cdot(n-\tau(c))

for
1\leq i\leq k
.
Suppose that
\text{rank}(M^{\prime}|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})\geq 3
.
Then either
|V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\cap V_{p}(P)|\leq O_{d,k,s}(p^{d-k-2})
or
V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\subseteq V_{p}(P)
.

Moreover, if
V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\subseteq V_{p}(P)
, then

P=Q+MP_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}L_{i}P_{i}

for some integer coefficient polynomials
P_{0},\dots,P_{k}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(P_{0})\leq s-2
and
\deg(P_{i})\leq s-1,1\leq i\leq k
, and for some
Q\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(Q)\leq s
.

In particular, the conclusion of this corollary holds if
d\geq k+\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})+3
, or if
d\geq 2k+3
.

Corollary 7.5

(Lifting of a special case of Proposition
6.8
)
.

Let
d,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{d,k}\delta^{-O_{d,k}(1)}
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a quadratic form of
p
-rank at least
s^{2}+s+3
, and
W
be a subset of
\Box_{p,s}(V(M))\cap([p]^{d})^{s+1}
of cardinality at least
\delta|\Box_{p,s}(V(M))\cap([p]^{d})^{s+1}|
. Let
P,Q\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p|\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(P)\leq k-1
and
\deg(Q)\leq k
. Suppose that for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W
, we have that

\Delta_{h_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}P(n)+\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}Q(n)\in\mathbb{Z},

(where
\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{2}}P(n)
is understood as
P(n)
when
s=1
). Then

P=MP_{1}+P_{2}+P_{3}\text{ and }Q=MQ_{1}+Q_{2}+Q_{3}

for some integer coefficient polynomials
P_{1}
and
Q_{1}
of degrees at most
k-3
and
k-2
respectively, some integer valued polynomials
P_{2}
and
Q_{2}
of degrees at most
k-1
and
k
respectively, and some
\mathbb{Z}/p
-valued polynomials
P_{3}
and
Q_{3}
of degrees at most
s-2
and
s-1
respectively.

Corollary 7.6

(Lifting of Proposition
6.12
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{d}\delta^{-O_{d}(1)}
be a prime, and
A
be a
\mathbb{Z}
-valued
d\times d
matrix of
p
-rank at least 3. Let
B
be a
\mathbb{Z}
-valued
d\times d
matrix and
v\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Let
W
be a subset of
[p]^{d}
of cardinality at least
\delta p^{d}
. Suppose that for all
w\in W
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
(nA)\cdot w\in p\mathbb{Z}
, we have that

(nB+v)\cdot w\in p\mathbb{Z}.

Then
v\in p\mathbb{Z}
and
B=cA+pB_{0}
for some
c\in\mathbb{Z}
and some
d\times d
integer valued matrix
B_{0}
.

7.3.
The
p
-expansion trick and applications

We also need to extend all the results in Section
7.2
from the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-setting to the
\mathbb{Q}
-setting, and thus cover all rational polynomials.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a quadratic form. Note that
\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
is the set of polynomials
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{R})
such that
M(n)\in\mathbb{Z}\Rightarrow f(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We first provide an explicit description for
\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
as well as some more general sets.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\gg_{d,k,s}1
be a prime number,

M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a non-degenerate quadratic form induced by some
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
associated with the matrix
A
,
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
with a basis
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
for some
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Denote

L_{j}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(h_{j}\tau(A))\cdot(n-\tau(c))

for
1\leq j\leq k
. Suppose that
\text{rank}(M^{\prime}|_{V^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}})\geq 3
.
The followings are equivalent:

(1)

f
belongs to
\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
and is of degree at most
s
;

(2)

we have

(7.1)

Q_{0}f=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}

for some
Q_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q_{0}\leq O_{d,s}(1)
and some integer valued polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
, and that
p^{\deg(f)}f
is an integer valued polynomial.

Moreover, if all the coefficients of
f
are in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
, then we may write

f=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s,i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq\min\{r,s\}}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}

for some integer coefficient polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
.

In particular, the conclusion of this proposition holds if
d\geq k+\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})+3
, or if
d\geq 2k+3
.

The approach of the proof of Proposition
7.7
is to decompose a
\mathbb{Q}
-valued polynomial as the sum of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{i}
-valued polynomials, and then repeatedly use results for
\mathbb{Z}/p
-valued polynomials to deduce similar results for the
\mathbb{Q}
-valued polynomial
f
.
To be more precise, for any polynomial
f
with rational coefficients, we may write
Qf=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\frac{f_{i}}{p^{i}}
for some
Q\in\mathbb{Z},p\nmid Q
and some “good” polynomials
f_{i}
. Here “good” means that
f_{i}
behaves very similar to a
\mathbb{Z}/p
-valued polynomial. One can then prove that periodicity conditions on
f
descents to similar conditions on
f_{k}
, leading to the conclusion that
\frac{f_{k}}{p}
is “good”. We may then absorb the term
\frac{f_{k}}{p^{k}}
by
\frac{f_{k-1}}{p^{k-1}}
and reduce
k
by 1. Inductively, we have
k=0
, which implies that
Qf
itself is “good”. For convenience we refer to this approach as the
p
-expansion trick
.

Proof of Proposition
7.7
.

If (ii) holds, then for all
n\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
, we have that
M(n),L_{1}(n),\dots,L_{k}(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
and thus
f(n)\in(\mathbb{Z}/Q_{0})\cap(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\deg(f)})=\mathbb{Z}
. So

f
belongs to
\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
.

We now assume that (i) holds. We a slight abuse of notation, the matrices associated with both
M
and
M^{\prime}
will be denoted by
A
.
Assume that

M(n)=\frac{1}{p}((n\tau(A))\cdot n+u\cdot n+v)

for some
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
v\in\mathbb{Z}
. Fix any
n\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
. Then
n+pm\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. So
f(n+pm)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. By the multivariate polynomial interpolation, it is not hard to see that
f
takes values in
\frac{1}{Qp^{s^{\prime}}}\mathbb{Z}
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q\leq O_{d,s}(1)
.
So there exists
Q_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q_{0}\leq O_{d,s}(1)
and some minimal
0\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
such that

Q_{0}f=\sum_{i=0}^{s^{\prime}}\frac{f_{i}}{p^{i}}

for some integer valued polynomials
f_{i}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}
of degree at most
s
.

We say that
g
is a
good
polynomial of
level
L
if

g=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s,i_{0}+\dots+i_{k}\leq L}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}

for some integer valued polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
be the smallest integer such that we can write
Q_{0}f
as

Q_{0}f=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\frac{f_{i}}{p^{i}}

for some good polynomial
f_{i}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}
of degree at most
s
and level at most
s^{\prime}-i
. Obviously such
k
exists and is at most
s^{\prime}
. Our goal is to show that
k=0
.

Suppose that

k>0
. Since
n\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\Rightarrow f(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have that

n\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\Rightarrow f_{k}(n)\in p\mathbb{Z}.

Suppose that

f_{k}=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s,i_{0}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s^{\prime}-k}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}

for some integer valued polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
.

Claim.

For any
n\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
with
2\iota(n)A+\iota(u),\iota(h_{1})A,\dots,\iota(h_{k})A
being linearly independent,
we have that
R_{i}(n)\in p\mathbb{Z}
for all
i
.

Fix
n\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
with
2\iota(n)A+\iota(u),\iota(h_{1})A,\dots,\iota(h_{k})A
being linearly independent. Note that
n+pm\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. So we have that
f_{k}(n+pm)\in p\mathbb{Z}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Since

\begin{split}M(n+pm)^{i_{0}}\equiv(M(n)+(2(nA)+u)\cdot m)^{i_{0}}\mod p\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}L_{j}(n+pm)^{i_{j}}\equiv(L_{j}(n)+(h_{j}A)\cdot m)^{i_{j}}\mod p\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for all
1\leq j\leq k
, the fact that
f_{k}(n+pm)\in p\mathbb{Z}

implies that

(7.2)

\begin{split}\sum_{(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s,i_{0}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s^{\prime}-k}R_{i}(n)(M(n)+(2(nA)+u)\cdot m)^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}(L_{j}(n)+(h_{j}A)\cdot m)^{i_{j}}\in p\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

Since
2\iota(n)A+\iota(u),\iota(h_{1})A,\dots,\iota(h_{k})A
are linearly independent, the map

m\mapsto(2(n\tau(A))+u)\cdot m,(h_{1}\tau(A))\cdot m,\dots,(h_{k}\tau(A))\cdot m)\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k+1}

is an injection from
\{0,\dots,p-1\}^{d}
to
\{0,\dots,p-1\}^{k+1}
. It then follows from (
7.2
) that

(7.3)

\begin{split}\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s,i_{0}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s^{\prime}-k}R_{i}(n)x_{0}^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}x_{j}^{i_{j}}\in p\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

for all
x_{0},\dots,x_{k+1}\in\mathbb{Z}
. viewing (
7.3
) as a polynomial in
x_{0},\dots,x_{k}
, we have that

QR_{i}(n)\in p\mathbb{Z}
for all
i
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q\leq O_{d,s}(1)
. Since
R_{i}(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
, we deduce that
R_{i}(n)\in(\frac{p}{Q}\mathbb{Z})\cap\mathbb{Z}=p\mathbb{Z}
. This proves the claim.

Let
W
be the set of
n\in V(M^{\prime})\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
such that
2\iota(n)A+\iota(u),\iota(h_{1})A,\dots,\iota(h_{k})A
are linearly dependent. Since
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are linearly independent and since
M^{\prime}
is non-degenerate,

2\iota(n)A+\iota(u),\iota(h_{1})A,\dots,\iota(h_{k})A
are linearly dependent if and only if
\iota(n)A+\iota(u)A^{-1}/2
belongs to the span of
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
. Recall that
V
is the span of
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
. We see that there exist
w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
W\subseteq(V\cap V^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}})+w
. So by Proposition
4.8
,

|W|\leq p^{\dim(V^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}})-\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}})}\leq p^{d-k-3}.

We may invoke Corollary
7.4
from the claim to conclude that

\frac{1}{p}R_{i}=R^{\prime}_{i}+MR_{i,0}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}R_{i,j}

for some integer valued polynomials
R_{i,0},\dots,R_{i,k}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(R_{i,0})\leq\deg(f)-2
and
\deg(R_{i,j})\leq\deg(f)-1,1\leq j\leq k
, and for some
R^{\prime}_{i}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(R^{\prime}_{i})\leq\deg(f)
.
This implies that
\frac{1}{p}f_{k}

is a good polynomial of degree at most
s
and of level at most
s^{\prime}-(k-1)
, which contradicts to the minimality of
k
. We have thus proved that
k=0
and thus
Q_{0}f
is a good polynomial of level at most
s^{\prime}
.

Therefore, we may write
Q_{0}f=\frac{1}{p^{\deg(f)}}g
for some integer valued polynomial
g
. To show that
(ii) holds, it suffices to show that
g(n)\in Q_{0}\mathbb{Z}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Fix any
n_{0}\in V_{p}(M)^{h_{1},\dots,h_{k}}
. For any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
n_{0}+pm\equiv n\mod(s!)^{d}Q_{0}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Since
g
is integer valued, we have that

g(n)\equiv g(n_{0}+pm)\mod Q_{0}\mathbb{Z}
. On the other hand, since
n_{0}+pm\in V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
, we have that
f(n_{0}+pm)=g(n_{0}+pm)/Q_{0}p^{\deg(f)}\in\mathbb{Z}
. So
g(n)\equiv g(n_{0}+pm)\equiv 0\mod Q_{0}\mathbb{Z}
.

If in addition all the coefficients of
f
are in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
, then we may set
s^{\prime}=\min\{r,s\}
in the previous discussion and deduce that
Q_{0}f
is a good polynomial of level at most
\min\{r,s\}
. Enlarging
Q_{0}
if necessary, we may write

Q_{0}f=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s,i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq\min\{r,s\}}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}

for some integer coefficient polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
.
Let
Q^{\ast}
be any integer with
Q^{\ast}Q_{0}\equiv 1\mod p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
. Then
(Q^{\ast}Q_{0}-1)f
is an integer coefficient polynomial, and thus the conclusion follows as
f=Q^{\ast}Q_{0}f+(Q^{\ast}Q_{0}-1)f
since the term
(Q^{\ast}Q_{0}-1)f
can be absorbed by
Q^{\ast}R_{(0,\dots,0)}
.
∎

As a special case of Proposition
7.7
, we have

Corollary 7.8
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a quadratic form of
p
-rank at least 3. If
p\gg_{d,s}1
, then the followings are equivalent:

(1)

f
belongs to
\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
and is of degree at most
s
;

(2)

we have

Q_{0}f=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}M^{i}R_{i}

for some
Q_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q_{0}\leq O_{d,s}(1)
and some integer valued polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-2i
.

Our next task is to use the
p
-expansion trick to generalize Corollary
7.5
.

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
d,K,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{d,k}\delta^{-O_{d,k}(1)}
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a quadratic form of
p
-rank at least
s^{2}+s+3
, and
W
be a subset of
\Box_{p,s}(V(M))\cap([p^{2}K]^{d})^{s+1}
of cardinality at least
\delta|\Box_{p,s}(V(M))\cap([p^{2}K]^{d})^{s+1}|
. Let
P,Q\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
with
\deg(P)\leq k-1
and
\deg(Q)\leq k
. Suppose that for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W+p^{2}K(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}
, we have that

\Delta_{h_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}P(n)+\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}Q(n)\in\mathbb{Z},

(where
\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{2}}P(n)
is understood as
P(n)
when
s=1
). Then

qP=P_{1}+P_{2}\text{ and }qQ=Q_{1}+Q_{2}

for some integer
q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
q\ll_{d,k}\delta^{-O_{d,k}(1)}
, some
P_{1},Q_{1}\in\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(P_{1})\leq k-1
,
\deg(Q_{1})\leq k
, and some
P_{2},Q_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
with
\deg(P_{2})\leq s-2
,
\deg(Q_{2})\leq s-1
.

Remark 7.10
.

The proof of Proposition
7.9
involves with a very intricate application of the
p
-expansion trick. However, such a generalization Corollary
7.5
is necessary in the study of equidistribution properties for polynomial sequences (Theorem
5.1
), where we need to deal with polynomial sequences which are apriori not necessarily periodic. See the footnote after (
8.20
) for details.

Proof of Proposition
7.9
.

Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{d,k}\delta^{-O_{d,k}(1)}
.
For convenience denote
d^{\prime}:=\text{rank}_{p}(M)\geq s^{2}+s+3
. We assume that
s\leq k+1
since otherwise one can simply take
P=P_{2},Q=Q_{2},q=1
and
P_{1}=Q_{1}=0
.
Since
P,Q
take values in
\mathbb{Q}
and are of degree at most
k
, by the multivariate polynomial interpolation,
there exists
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
and
t_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

qP=\sum_{j=0}^{t_{0}}\frac{P^{\prime}_{j}}{p^{j}}\text{ and }qQ=\sum_{j=0}^{t_{0}}\frac{Q^{\prime}_{j}}{p^{j}}

for some integer coefficient polynomials
P^{\prime}_{j},Q^{\prime}_{j}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
of with
\deg(P^{\prime}_{j})\leq k-1
and
\deg(Q^{\prime}_{j})\leq k
.
We say that a polynomial
f\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}
is
good
if

f=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\deg(f)/2\rfloor}M^{j}f_{j}

for some integer coefficient polynomial
f_{j}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-2j
.
Clearly
P^{\prime}_{0},\dots,P^{\prime}_{t_{0}},Q^{\prime}_{0},\dots,Q^{\prime}_{t_{0}}
are good polynomials.

Let
t\in\mathbb{N}
be the smallest integer such that we can write

q^{\prime}P=\sum_{j=0}^{t}\frac{P^{\prime\prime}_{j}}{p^{j}}+P^{\prime}\text{ and }q^{\prime}Q=\sum_{j=0}^{t}\frac{Q^{\prime\prime}_{j}}{p^{j}}+Q^{\prime}

for some
q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q^{\prime}
, some good polynomials
P^{\prime\prime}_{j},Q^{\prime\prime}_{j}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
of with
\deg(P^{\prime\prime}_{j})\leq k-1
,
\deg(Q^{\prime\prime}_{j})\leq k
and some
P^{\prime},Q^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of with
\deg(P^{\prime})\leq k-2
and
\deg(Q^{\prime})\leq k-1
. Obviously such
t
exists and is at most
t_{0}
. Our first goal is to show that
t=0
.

Suppose on the contrary that
t>0
.
We consider the last terms and write

P^{\prime\prime}_{t}=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor(k-1)/2\rfloor}M^{j}P_{j}\text{ and }Q^{\prime\prime}_{t}=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}M^{j}Q_{j}

for some integer coefficient polynomials
P_{j},Q_{j}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(P_{j})\leq k-1-2j
and
\deg(Q_{j})\leq k-2j
.
We say that a polynomial
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
is
simple
if

q^{\prime}g=pg_{0}+pMg_{1}+g_{2}

for some
q^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},q^{\prime}\leq O_{k,s}(1)
, some
g_{0}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(g_{0})\leq\deg(g)
, some
integer coefficient polynomials
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(g_{0})\leq\deg(g)-2
, and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p|\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(g_{0})\leq\deg(g)-1
. If we can show that all of
P_{0},\dots,P_{\lfloor(k-1)/2\rfloor},Q_{0},\dots,Q_{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
are simple, then enlarging the constant
q^{\prime}
if necessary, we may absorb the term
\frac{P^{\prime\prime}_{t}}{p^{t}}
by
\frac{P^{\prime\prime}_{t-1}}{p^{t-1}}+P^{\prime}
, and the term
\frac{Q^{\prime\prime}_{t}}{p^{t}}
by
\frac{Q^{\prime\prime}_{t-1}}{p^{t-1}}+Q^{\prime}
, which contradicts to the minimality of
t
.

By induction,
it then suffices to show that if all of
P_{i+1},\dots,P_{\lfloor k/2\rfloor},Q_{i+1},\dots,Q_{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
are simple for some
0\leq i\leq\lfloor k/2\rfloor
, then
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
are also simply (for the special case
i=\lfloor k/2\rfloor
, we show that
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
are simply without any induction hypothesis).

Since
P_{i+1},\dots,P_{\lfloor k/2\rfloor},Q_{i+1},\dots,Q_{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
are simple, enlarging the constant
q^{\prime}
and
absorbing the term
\frac{1}{p^{t}}\sum_{j=i+1}^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}M^{j}P_{j}
by
\frac{P^{\prime\prime}_{t-1}}{p^{t-1}}+P^{\prime}
, and the term
\frac{1}{p^{t}}\sum_{j=i+1}^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}M^{j}Q_{j}
by
\frac{Q^{\prime\prime}_{t-1}}{p^{t-1}}+Q^{\prime}
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that

P^{\prime\prime}_{t}=\sum_{j=0}^{i}M^{j}P_{j}\text{ and }Q^{\prime\prime}_{t}=\sum_{j=0}^{i}M^{j}Q_{j}.

By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
W_{0}
of
\Box_{p,s}(V(M))\cap([p]^{d})^{s+1}
of cardinality at least
\delta|\Box_{p,s}(V(M))\cap([p]^{d})^{s+1}|/2
and for each
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}
a set
W_{0}(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\subseteq([pK]^{d})^{s+1}
of cardinality at least
\delta(pK)^{d(s+1)}/2
such that
(n+pm_{0},h_{1}+pm_{1},\dots,h_{s}+pm_{s})\in W
for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}
and
(m_{0},\dots,m_{s})\in W_{0}(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})
.

Fix any
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}
and
(m_{0},\dots,m_{s})\in W_{0}(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})
.
We have

\Delta_{h_{s-1}+pm_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}+pm_{1}}P(n)+\Delta_{h_{s}+pm_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}+pm_{1}}Q(n)\in\mathbb{Z}

by assumption. So

(7.4)

\begin{split}\Delta_{h_{s-1}+pm_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}+pm_{1}}P^{\prime\prime}_{t}(n)+\Delta_{h_{s}+pm_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}+pm_{1}}Q^{\prime\prime}_{t}(n)\in p\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

Assume that

M(n)=\frac{1}{p}((nA)\cdot n+u\cdot n+v)

for some
d\times d
symmetric integer coefficient matrix
A
, some
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and some
v\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then for all
m,n\in V_{p}(M)
, we have

(7.5)

\begin{split}M(n+pm)\equiv M(n)+((2(nA)+u)\cdot m)\mod p\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

For any
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{r})\in\{0,1\}^{r}
and
a=(a_{1},\dots,a_{r})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{r}
, denote

\epsilon\cdot a:=\epsilon_{1}a_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{r}a_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

Since
P_{j}
and
Q_{j}
have integer coefficients, it is not hard to see from (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) that any
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}
and
(m_{0},\dots,m_{s})\in W_{0}(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})
, we have

(7.6)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{\epsilon_{\ast}\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}(-1)^{|\epsilon_{\ast}|}(M(n^{\prime}+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})+(2(n^{\prime}+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})A+u)\cdot(\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot m_{\ast}))^{j}P_{j}(n^{\prime}+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s}}(-1)^{|\epsilon|}(M(n^{\prime}+\epsilon\cdot h)+(2(n^{\prime}+\epsilon\cdot h)A+u)\cdot(\epsilon\cdot m))^{j}Q_{j}(n^{\prime}+\epsilon\cdot h)\in\mathbb{Z},\end{split}

where
n^{\prime}=n+pm_{0}
,
h=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s})
,
h_{\ast}=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})
,
m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{s})
, and
m_{\ast}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{s-1})
, and the terms
\epsilon_{\ast},h_{\ast},m_{\ast}
are regarded as non-existing if
s=1
.
Since
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
have integer coefficients, the residue class mod
\mathbb{Z}
of the left hand side of (
7.6
) remains unchanged if we replace
m_{i}
by
m_{i}\mod p\mathbb{Z}
for
0\leq i\leq s
.
In order words, for fixed
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}
, the left hand side of (
7.6
) is a
p
-periodic polynomial in the variables
(m_{0},\dots,m_{s})
.
Since
W_{0}(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\mod p(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}
contains at least
\delta p^{d(s+1)}/2
residue classes mod
p\mathbb{Z}
, it follows from Lemma
4.10
that (
7.6
) holds for all
m_{0},\dots,m_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}
(and thus for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}+p(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}
).

Suppose that
2nA+u,h_{1}A,\dots,h_{s}A
are
p
-linearly independent. It is not hard to see that for any
1\leq j\leq i
and
c_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, there exists
m_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that

(2nA+u)\cdot m_{j},(h_{j^{\prime}}A)\cdot m_{j},(h_{i+j}A)\cdot m_{j}-c_{j}\in p\mathbb{Z}

for all
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq s,j^{\prime}\neq i+j
.
For
i+1\leq j\leq s
, let
m_{j}=\mathbb{0}
.

Then for all
0\leq j\leq i
, and
\epsilon_{\ast}\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}
, we have that

(7.7)

\begin{split}&\quad(M(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})+(2(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})A+u)\cdot(\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot m_{\ast}))^{j}P_{j}(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})\\
&=(M(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})+(2(n+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot h^{\prime}+\epsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}\cdot h^{\prime\prime}_{\ast})A+u)\cdot(\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot m^{\prime}))^{j}P_{j}(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})\\
&\equiv\Bigl(M(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{i}\epsilon_{\ell}\epsilon_{i+\ell}c_{\ell}\Bigr)^{j}P_{j}(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})\mod p\mathbb{Z},\end{split}

where
h^{\prime}:=(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})
,
h^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}:=(h_{i+1},\dots,h_{s-1})
,
\epsilon^{\prime}:=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{i})
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}:=(\epsilon_{i+1},\dots,\epsilon_{s-1})
.
Similarly, for all
0\leq j\leq i
, and
\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s}
, we have that

(7.8)

\begin{split}&\quad(M(n+\epsilon\cdot h)+(2(n+\epsilon\cdot h)A+u)\cdot(\epsilon\cdot m))^{j}Q_{j}(n+\epsilon\cdot h)\\
&\equiv\Bigl(M(n+\epsilon\cdot h)+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{i}\epsilon_{\ell}\epsilon_{i+\ell}c_{\ell}\Bigr)^{j}Q_{j}(n+\epsilon\cdot h)\mod p\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

Combining (
7.6
), (
7.7
) and (
7.8
), we have that

(7.9)

\begin{split}&\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{\epsilon_{\ast}\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}(-1)^{|\epsilon_{\ast}|}\Bigl(M(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{i}\epsilon_{\ell}\epsilon_{i+\ell}c_{\ell}\Bigr)^{j}P_{j}(n+\epsilon_{\ast}\cdot h_{\ast})\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s}}(-1)^{|\epsilon|}\Bigl(M(n+\epsilon\cdot h)+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{i}\epsilon_{\ell}\epsilon_{i+\ell}c_{\ell}\Bigr)^{j}Q_{j}(n+\epsilon\cdot h)\in p\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Viewing (
7.9
) as a polynomial of
c_{1},\dots,c_{i}
and considering the coefficient of the term
c_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot c_{i}
, by interpolation, we have that

\begin{split}&q^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\ast}\in\{0,1\}^{s-2i-1}}(-1)^{|\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\ast}|}P_{i}(n+h_{1}+\dots+h_{2i}+\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\ast}\cdot h^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\ast})\\
&+q^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{s-2i}}(-1)^{|\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}|}Q_{i}(n+h_{1}+\dots+h_{2i}+\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}\cdot h^{\prime\prime\prime})\in p\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for some
q^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},q^{\prime\prime}\leq O_{d,k}(1)
,
where
h^{\prime\prime\prime}=(h_{2i+1},\dots,h_{s}),

h^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\ast}=(h_{2i+1},\dots,h_{s-1})
,
\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\ast}=(\epsilon_{2i+1},\dots,\epsilon_{s-1})
and
\epsilon^{\prime\prime\prime}=(\epsilon_{2i+1},\dots,\epsilon_{s})
, and the sum

\sum_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{r}}(-1)^{|\epsilon|}a(n+\epsilon\cdot h)

is considered as
a(n)
if
r=0
, and as
0
if
r<0
.
In conclusion, we have that

(7.10)

\begin{split}q^{\prime\prime}(\Delta_{h_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{2i+1}}P_{i}(n^{\prime})+\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{2i+1}}Q_{i}(n^{\prime}))\in p\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

with
n^{\prime}=n+h_{1}+\dots+h_{2i}

for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W_{0}+p(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}
such that
2nA+u,h_{1}A,\dots,h_{s}A
are
p
-linearly independent.

It is convenient to work in the
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
-setting in the rest of the proof.
Let
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the non-degenerate quadratic form induced by
M
, and let
W^{\prime}
denote the set of
(n^{\prime},h_{2i+1},\dots,h_{s})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s-2i+1}
such that
n^{\prime}=n+h_{1}+\dots+h_{2i}
for some
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\iota(W_{0})
such that
2n\iota(A)+\iota(u),h_{1}\iota(A),\dots,h_{s}\iota(A)
are linearly independent.
Then it is not hard to see that (
7.10
) holds for all
(n^{\prime},h_{2i+1},\dots,h_{s})\in\iota^{-1}(W^{\prime})
. If we can show that
|W^{\prime}|\geq\delta|\Box_{s-2i}(V(\tilde{M}))|/8
, then it follows from (
7.10
) and Corollary
7.5
that
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
are simply.

We now estimate the cardinality of
W^{\prime}
.
Since
d^{\prime}\geq s^{2}+s+3
, it follows from Proposition
4.19
that

(7.11)

|\Box_{j}(V(\tilde{M}))|=p^{(j+1)d-(\frac{j(j+1)}{2}+1)}(1+O_{j}(p^{-1/2}))

for all
0\leq j\leq s
.
For convenience denote
\mathbb{x}:=(n^{\prime},h_{2i+1},\dots,h_{s})
. We say that
\mathbb{x}
is
good
if
2n^{\prime}\iota(A)+\iota(u),h_{2i+1}\iota(A),\dots,h_{s}\iota(A)
are linearly independent. Let
W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}
denote the set of
(h_{1},\dots,h_{2i})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2i}
such that
(n^{\prime}-h_{1}-\dots-h_{2i},h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\iota(W)
. Then

(7.12)

\sum_{\mathbb{x}\in\Box_{s-2i}(V(\tilde{M}))}|W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}|=|W_{0}|\geq\delta|\Box_{s}(V(\tilde{M}))|/2=\frac{1}{2}\delta p^{(s+1)d-(\frac{s(s+1)}{2}+1)}(1+O_{s}(p^{-1/2})).

Therefore, informally speaking, by (
7.11
) and (
7.12
), on average the cardinality of
W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}
is at least
\delta p^{2id-(2s-2i+1)i}/2
.

Note that for good
\mathbb{x}
,
(h_{1},\dots,h_{2i})\in W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}
only if
(n^{\prime}-h_{1}-\dots-h_{2i},h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(\tilde{M}))
. Using Lemma
4.18
, this is equivalent of saying that

•

\tilde{M}(n^{\prime})-\tilde{M}(n^{\prime}-h_{j})=(2n^{\prime}\iota(A)+\iota(u))\cdot h_{j}=0
for all
1\leq j\leq 2i
;

•

(h_{j}\iota(A))\cdot h_{j^{\prime}}=0
for all
1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq s
with at least one of
j,j^{\prime}
belonging to
\{1,\dots,2i\}
.

There are in total
(2s-2i+1)i\leq(s+1/2)^{2}/2
independent equations above in total.
15
15

15

Using the terminology in Appendix
B
, this means that
W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}
is a subset of an
\tilde{M}
-set of total co-dimension
(2s-2i+1)i
.
Since
d\geq s^{2}+s+3
, by repeatedly using Corollary
4.16
and using the fact that
\mathbb{x}
is good, it is not difficult to show that

(7.13)

|W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}|\leq p^{2id-(2s-2i+1)i}(1+O_{s}(p^{-1/2})).

Here we will not present the proof for (
7.13
), as this is a special case of Theorem
C.3
.

Let
V:=\{n\iota(A)\colon n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Then
V
is a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
d^{\prime}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be the set of
m,m_{2i+1},\dots,m_{s}\in V
such that
2m+\iota(u),m_{2i+1},\dots,m_{s}
are linearly dependent. Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.11
(i), it is not hard to see the
|V^{\prime}|\leq sp^{(d+1)(s-2i)}
. On the other hand, for each
m\in V
, the number of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
n\iota(A)=m
is at most
p^{d-d^{\prime}}
. Therefore, the number of
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s-2i+1}
which is not good is at most

p^{d-d^{\prime}}|V^{\prime}|\leq sp^{(s-2i+1)d+(s-2i)-d^{\prime}}.
So

(7.14)

\sum_{\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s-2i+1},\text{ $\mathbb{x}$ is not good}}|W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}|\leq sp^{(s-2i+1)d+(s-2i)-d^{\prime}}\cdot p^{2id}=sp^{(s+1)d+(s-2i)-d^{\prime}}.

Since
(s+1)d+(s-2i)-d^{\prime}<(s+1)d-(\frac{s(s+1)}{2}+1)
, it follows from (
7.11
), (
7.12
), (
7.13
), (
7.14
) and the Pigeonhole Principle that
there exists a subset
W^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\Box_{s-2i}(V(\tilde{M}))
of cardinality at least
\delta|\Box_{s-2i}(V(\tilde{M}))|/20
such that
|W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}|\geq\delta p^{2id-(2s-2i+1)i}/20
for all
\mathbb{x}\in W^{\prime\prime}
. In particular,
W^{\prime}_{\mathbb{x}}
is non-empty for all
\mathbb{x}\in W^{\prime\prime}
.
Therefore,
W^{\prime\prime}
is a subset of
W^{\prime}
and thus

|W^{\prime}|\geq|W^{\prime\prime}|\geq\delta|\Box_{s-2i}(V(\tilde{M}))|/20
.

In conclusion, we have shown by induction that
t=0
and therefore we may write

qP=P^{\prime}_{0}\text{ and }qQ=Q^{\prime}_{0}

for some good polynomials
P^{\prime}_{0},Q^{\prime}_{0}
of degrees at most
k-1
and
k
respectively.
Our final goal is to show that one can further require
q
to be small.

Let
q^{\ast}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be such that
qq^{\ast}\equiv 1\mod p^{k}\mathbb{Z}
. Then

P=\frac{qq^{\ast}-1}{q}P^{\prime}_{0}+q^{\ast}P^{\prime}_{0}
.
Denote
P_{0}:=(qq^{\ast}-1)P^{\prime}_{0}
. Since
P^{\prime}_{0}
is good, we have that
P_{0}
has coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}

and that
\Delta_{h_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}(q^{\ast}P^{\prime}_{0})(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{p,s}(V(M))
.
Similarly,
Q_{0}:=(qq^{\ast}-1)Q^{\prime}_{0}
also has coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}
, and
\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}(q^{\ast}Q^{\prime}_{0})(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{p,s}(V(M))
.
So we have that

\Delta_{h_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}P_{0}(n)+\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}Q_{0}(n)\in q\mathbb{Z}

for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in W+([pK^{\prime}]^{d})^{s+1}
, where for convenience we denote
K^{\prime}:=pK
.

By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists some
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{p,s}(V(M))\cap([p]^{d})^{s+1}
and a subset
J\subseteq([K^{\prime}]^{d})^{s+1}
of cardinality at least
\delta{K^{\prime}}^{d(s+1)}
such that
(n+pm_{0},h_{1}+pm_{1},\dots,h_{s}+pm_{s})\in W
for all
(m_{0},\dots,m_{s})\in J
.
Write

G(m_{0},m_{1},\dots,m_{s}):=\Delta_{h_{s-1}+pm_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}+pm_{1}}P_{0}(n+pm_{0})+\Delta_{h_{s}+pm_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}+pm_{1}}Q_{0}(n+pm_{0}).

Then
G
is a rational polynomial of degree at most
k
.
By assumption,

\frac{1}{q}G(\mathbb{m})\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
\mathbb{m}\in J+([K^{\prime}]^{d})^{s+1}
.
So by Proposition
3.20
, there exists
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
r\ll_{d,k,s}\delta^{-O_{d,k,s}(1)}
such that all the coefficients of
\frac{1}{q}G
are in
\mathbb{Z}/r
.
Write

\begin{split}&\quad G^{\prime}(m_{0},m_{1},\dots,m_{s}):=\frac{1}{q}G\Bigl(\frac{1}{p}m_{0}-n,\frac{1}{p}m_{1}-h_{1},\dots,\frac{1}{p}m_{s}-h_{s}\Bigr)\\
&=\Delta_{m_{s-1}}\dots\Delta_{m_{1}}(\frac{1}{q}P_{0})(m_{0})+\Delta_{m_{s}}\dots\Delta_{m_{1}}(\frac{1}{q}Q_{0})(m_{0}).\end{split}

Then the coefficients of
G^{\prime}
are in
\mathbb{Z}/rp^{k}
. On the other hand, since
P_{0}
and
Q_{0}
are integer coefficient polynomials, the coefficients of
G^{\prime}
are in
\mathbb{Z}/q
and are thus in
(\mathbb{Z}/rp^{k})\cap(\mathbb{Z}/q)\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/r
.

Now assume that

\frac{1}{q}P_{0}(n):=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|i|\leq k}a_{i}n^{i}\text{ and }\frac{1}{q}Q_{0}(n):=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|i|\leq k}b_{i}n^{i}

for some
a_{i},b_{i}\in\mathbb{Q}
.
Fix any
i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|i|\geq s-1
.
Let
(m_{1},\dots,m_{|i|+1})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|i|}
be any vector such that
m_{s}=\mathbb{0}

and that
the number of
m_{i^{\prime}},1\leq i^{\prime}\leq|i|+1,i^{\prime}\neq s
which equals to the
j
-th standard unit vector is equal to
i_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq d
. Then it is not hard to see that

\Delta_{m_{|i|+1}}\dots\Delta_{m_{s+1}}G^{\prime}(\mathbb{0},m_{1},\dots,m_{s})=i!a_{i}.

Since the coefficients of
G^{\prime}
are in
\mathbb{Z}/r
, we have that
a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}/r^{\prime}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|i|\geq s-1
, where
r^{\prime}:=(k!)^{d}r
.

Now fix any
i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|i|\geq s
.
Let
(m_{1},\dots,m_{|i|})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|i|}
be any vector such that the number of
m_{i^{\prime}},1\leq i^{\prime}\leq|i|
which equals to the
j
-th standard unit vector is equal to
i_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq d
. Then it is not hard to see that

\Delta_{m_{|i|}}\dots\Delta_{m_{s+1}}G^{\prime}(\mathbb{0},m_{1},\dots,m_{s})=i!b_{i}+(i-m_{s})!a_{i-m_{s}}.

Since the coefficients of
G^{\prime}
are in
\mathbb{Z}/r
and since
a_{i-m_{s}}\in\mathbb{Z}/r^{\prime}
, we have that
b_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}/r^{\prime}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|i|\geq s
.
So we may write
P_{0}
as
\frac{1}{q}P_{0}=P_{1}+P_{2}
for some polynomial
P_{1}
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}/r^{\prime}
having at most the same degree as
P
and some
P_{2}
of degree at most
s-2
. So

r^{\prime}P=r^{\prime}(q^{\ast}P^{\prime}_{0}+P_{1})+r^{\prime}P_{2}

where
r^{\prime}(q^{\ast}P^{\prime}_{0}+P_{1})
take integer values in
V_{p}(M)
. A similar decomposition apples to
Q
and so we are done.
∎

By a change of variable trick, we use Proposition
7.9
to deduce the follow result:

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
d,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+},k\in\mathbb{N}
,

p\gg_{d}1
be a prime,
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,

V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
with a basis
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
for some
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form with
\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})\geq s^{2}+s+3
, and
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
with
\deg(g)\leq s
. Suppose that for all
(n_{0},n_{1},\dots,n_{s})\in\iota^{-1}(\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)))
, we have that
\Delta_{n_{s}}\dots\Delta_{n_{1}}g(n_{0})\in\mathbb{Z}.
Then

qg=g_{1}+g_{2}

for some integer
q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
q\ll_{d}1
, some
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c))\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(g_{1})\leq s
, and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
with
\deg(g_{2})\leq s-1
.

Proof.

If
k=0
, then the conclusion follows from Proposition
7.9
. So now we assume that
k\geq 1
.
Since
\Delta_{h_{s}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)=0
whenever
\deg(g)<s
. We may assume without loss of generality that
g
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
s
.
Let
A
be the matrix associated to
M
, and
\tilde{M}
be the regular liftings of
M
.
Let
M_{0}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
M_{0}(n):=(nA)\cdot n
.
Since
M
is non-degenerate,
V^{\perp_{M}}
is of dimension
r:=d-k
.
Let
v_{1},\dots,v_{r}
be a basis of
V^{\perp_{M}}
and
\phi\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{r}\to V^{\perp_{M}}
be the bijective linear transformation with
\phi(e_{i}):=v_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq r
. Let
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{r}\times\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
M^{\prime}(m,n):=M(\phi(m)+c)
for all
m\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{r}
and
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Suppose that
\phi(m):=mB
for some
r\times d
matrix
B
. Then
BAB^{T}
is the matrix associated to
M^{\prime}
.
Pick any
\tilde{v}_{1},\dots,\tilde{v}_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
\iota(\tilde{v}_{i})=v_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
, and let
\tilde{\phi}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{r}\to\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be the linear map given by
\tilde{\phi}(e_{i}):=\tilde{v}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq r
. It is clear that
\iota(\tilde{V})=V
and
\iota\circ\tilde{\phi}=\phi\circ\iota
.

Let
\tilde{c}:=\tau(c)
and
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{r+d}\to\mathbb{Q})
be the polynomial defined by
g^{\prime}(m,n):=g(\tilde{\phi}(m)+pn+\tilde{c})
.
It is not hard to check that for all
((n_{0}^{\prime},n_{0}^{\prime\prime}),(n^{\prime}_{1},n^{\prime\prime}_{1}),\dots,(n^{\prime}_{s},n^{\prime\prime}_{s}))\in\iota^{-1}(\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime})))
, we have that
(\tilde{\phi}(n_{0}^{\prime})+pn_{0}^{\prime\prime}+\tilde{c},\tilde{\phi}(n^{\prime}_{1})+pn^{\prime\prime}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\phi}(n^{\prime}_{s})+pn^{\prime\prime}_{s})\in\iota^{-1}(\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c)))
.
By assumption, we have that

\Delta_{(n^{\prime}_{s},n^{\prime\prime}_{s})}\dots\Delta_{(n^{\prime}_{1},n^{\prime\prime}_{1})}g^{\prime}(n_{0}^{\prime},n_{0}^{\prime\prime})=\Delta_{\tilde{\phi}(n^{\prime}_{s})+pn^{\prime\prime}_{s}}\dots\Delta_{\tilde{\phi}(n^{\prime}_{1})+pn^{\prime\prime}_{1}}g(\tilde{\phi}(n_{0}^{\prime})+pn_{0}^{\prime\prime}+\tilde{c})\in\mathbb{Z}.

Since
\text{rank}(M^{\prime})=\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})\geq s^{2}+s+3
,
by Proposition
7.9
, we may write

(7.15)

qg^{\prime}=g^{\prime}_{1}+g^{\prime}_{2}

for some integer
q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
q\ll_{d}1
, some
g^{\prime}_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime}))\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
with
\deg(g^{\prime}_{1})\leq s
, and some
g^{\prime}_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
with
\deg(g^{\prime}_{2})\leq s-1
.
Let
\tilde{M}^{\prime}
be the regular liftings of
M^{\prime}
.
Since
g^{\prime}_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime}))\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})=\text{poly}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
, by Corollary
7.8
, enlarging
q
if necessary, we may assume that
g^{\prime}_{1}
is of the form

g^{\prime}_{1}=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}R_{i}\tilde{M^{\prime}}^{i}

for some integer coefficient polynomial
R_{i}
of degree at most
s-2i
.
Since
g
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
s
, comparing the leading coefficients on both sides of (
7.15
), we have that

(7.16)

qg(\tilde{\phi}(m)+pn)=\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}R^{\prime}_{i}(m,n)(\frac{1}{p}(m\tau(BAB^{T}))\cdot m)^{i}

for some integer coefficient homogeneous polynomial
R_{i}
of degree
s-2i
.
Note that if
n_{\ast}\in\iota^{-1}(V(M_{0})\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
, then
\iota(n_{\ast})=\phi(m_{0})
for some
m_{0}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{r}
. Then there exist
(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{r+d}
such that
n_{\ast}=\tilde{\phi}(m)+pn
and that
\iota(m)=\phi(m_{0})
. Since
\phi(m_{0})\in V(M_{0})
, we have that
(\phi(m_{0})A)\cdot\phi(m_{0})=(m_{0}BAB^{T})\cdot m_{0}=0
. So
\frac{1}{p}(m\tau(BAB^{T}))\cdot m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Since
R^{\prime}_{i}(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}
, it follows from (
7.16
) that
qg(n_{\ast})=qg(\tilde{\phi}(m)+pn)\in\mathbb{Z}
.

In other words, we have that
qg\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M_{0})\cap V^{\perp_{M}})\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
. By Proposition
7.7
, enlarging
q
again if necessary, we may write
qf
as

(7.17)

qg=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s}R_{i}\tilde{M}_{0}^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{0,j}^{i_{j}}

for some integer valued polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
s-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
, where
\tilde{M}_{0}
is the regular lifting of
M_{0}
and

L_{0,j}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(h_{j}\tau(A))\cdot n.

Denote

L_{j}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(h_{j}\tau(A))\cdot(n-\tau(c)).

It then follows from (
7.17
) that we may write
qg
as

qg:=g_{1}+g_{2}:=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}+g_{2}

for some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s-1
. Again by Proposition
7.7
, we have that
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c))\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
.
We are done.
∎

As a consequence of Proposition
7.11
, we have the following result, which will be used in
[
18
,
19
]
:

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
d,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+},k\in\mathbb{N}
,

p\gg_{d}1
be a prime,
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,

V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
with a basis
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
for some
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form with
\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})\geq s^{2}+s+3
associated with a matrix
A
. Let
M_{0}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
M_{0}(n):=(nA)\cdot n
.
Then for any
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s
, the followings are equivalent:

(1)

\Delta_{n_{s}}\dots\Delta_{n_{1}}g(n_{0})\in\frac{1}{q}\mathbb{Z}
for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
for all
(n_{0},n_{1},\dots,n_{s})\in\Box_{p,s}(\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)))
;

(2)

we have

g=\frac{1}{q}g_{1}+g_{2}

for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
,
some
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c))\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s
and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s-1
;

(3)

we have

g=\frac{1}{q}g_{1}+g_{2}

for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
,
some
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M_{0})\cap V^{\perp_{M}})\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s
, and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s-1
.

Moreover, if
g
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
s
, then we may require
g_{2}=0
in (iii).

Proof.

Clearly (ii) implies (i). Similar to the argument at the end of Proposition
7.11
, it follows from Proposition
7.7
that
(ii) is equivalent to (iii). The part (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) is a consequence of Proposition
7.11
.

If in addition
g
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
s
, then in condition (iii), we may express
g_{1}
using Proposition
7.7
and then compare the leading terms on both sides of condition (iii) to conclude that one can set
g_{2}=0
.
∎

8.
Proof of the main equidistribution theorem

8.1.
Some preliminary reductions

We prove Theorem
5.1
in this section.
We start with a technical lemma on the connections between subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and those of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Let
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
d^{\prime}
and
L\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime}}\to V
be a bijective linear transformation. Let
\tilde{V}
be the
\mathbb{Z}
-span of
\tau(L(e_{1})),\dots,\tau(L(e_{d^{\prime}}))
and
\tilde{L}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}\to\tilde{V}
be the bijective linear transformation given by

\tilde{L}(n_{1},\dots,n_{d^{\prime}}):=n_{1}\tau(L(e_{1}))+\dots+n_{d^{\prime}}\tau(L(e_{d^{\prime}})).

In other words,
\tilde{L}/p
is the regular lifting of
L
.
Then it is clear that
\iota\circ\tilde{L}=L\circ\iota
and
\tilde{L}\circ\tau\equiv\tau\circ L\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Let

B
be a subset of
V+c
. It is clear that there is a bijective between
L^{-1}(B-c)
and
B
given by
z\mapsto L(z)+c
. However, the map
z\mapsto\tilde{L}(z)+\tau(c)
is not necessarily a bijection between
\iota^{-1}(L^{-1}(B-c))
and
\iota^{-1}(B)
. In order to connect the sets
\iota^{-1}(L^{-1}(B-c))
and
\iota^{-1}(B)
by
\tilde{L}
, we define a map

\Phi_{L,c}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}^{d}
as

\Phi_{L,c}(z,w):=\tau(c)+\tilde{L}(z)+pw.

Lemma 8.1
.

For all
K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the map
\Phi_{L,c}\mod pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is a
{K^{\prime}}^{d}
-to-1 map between
(\iota^{-1}(L^{-1}(B-c))\cap[pK^{\prime}]^{d^{\prime}})\times[K]^{d}
and
\iota^{-1}(B)\cap[pK]^{d}
.

Proof.

For convenience denote
\Phi:=\Phi_{L,c}
.
Let
(z,w)\in\iota^{-1}(L^{-1}(B-c))\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Then

\iota\circ\Phi(z,w)=\iota(\tau(c)+\tilde{L}(z)+pw)=c+\iota\circ\tilde{L}(z)=c+L\circ\iota(z)\in B.

So the range of
\Phi\mod pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is contained in
\iota^{-1}(B)\cap[pK]^{d}
.

Now for all
n\in\iota^{-1}(B)
, We may pick some
z^{\prime}\in L^{-1}(B-c)
such that
\iota(n)=L(z^{\prime})+c
. Let
z=\tau(z^{\prime})
. Then
z
belongs to
\iota^{-1}(L^{-1}(B-c))\cap[p]^{d^{\prime}}
and

\tilde{L}(z)+\tau(c)=\tilde{L}\circ\tau(z^{\prime})+\tau(c)\equiv\tau\circ L(z^{\prime})+\tau(c)=\tau\circ\iota(n)\equiv n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

So there exists
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
n=\tilde{L}(z)+\tau(c)+pw.
Therefore,
\Phi(z,w\mod K\mathbb{Z}^{d})\equiv n\mod pk\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
So the map
\Phi\mod pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is surjective.

Finally, for
(z,w),(z^{\prime},w^{\prime})\in(\iota^{-1}(L^{-1}(B-c))\cap[pK^{\prime}]^{d^{\prime}})\times[K]^{d}
, we have that
\Phi(z,w)\equiv\Phi(z^{\prime},w^{\prime})\mod pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
if and only if

\tilde{L}(z-z^{\prime})+p(w-w^{\prime})\in pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. If this is the case, then
\tilde{L}(z-z^{\prime})\in p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and thus
\mathbb{0}=\iota\circ\tilde{L}(z-z^{\prime})=L\circ\iota(z-z^{\prime})
. Since
L
is injective, this implies that
\iota(z-z^{\prime})=\mathbb{0}
and thus
z\equiv z^{\prime}\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
. Now for any such
z-z^{\prime}
and any fixed
w
, there is a unique
w^{\prime}\in[K]^{d}
such that
\frac{1}{p}\tilde{L}(z-z^{\prime})+(w-w^{\prime})\in K\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Since for each
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
, the number of
z^{\prime}\in[pK^{\prime}]^{d}
for which
z\equiv z^{\prime}\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
is equal to
{K^{\prime}}^{d}
. So
\Phi\mod pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is a
{K^{\prime}}^{d}
-to-1 map.
∎

Before proving Theorem
5.1
,
we start with some simplifications.
Suppose first that the conclusion holds for
V+c=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. For the general case, let
L\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}\to V
be a bijective linear transformation. Let
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic map given by
M^{\prime}(z):=M(L(z)+c)
.
Then it is clear that
L^{-1}((V(M)\cap(V+c))-c)=V(M^{\prime})
.

Let
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
be rational. By Corollary
3.21
, we may assume that
g
is
pK
-periodic for some
K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Then for any function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
, it follows from Lemma
8.1
(setting
B=V(M)\cap(V+c)
and
K^{\prime}=K
) that

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}F(g(n)\Gamma)=\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(n)\Gamma)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{z\in\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime}))\cap[pK]^{d-r}}\mathbb{E}_{w\in[K]^{d}}F(g(\tau(c)+\tilde{L}(z)+pw)\Gamma)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(z,w)\in\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime\prime}))\cap[pK]^{2d-r}}F(g(\tau(c)+\tilde{L}(z)+pw)\Gamma)\end{split}

where
M^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{2d-r}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is the quadratic form given by

M^{\prime\prime}(z,w):=M^{\prime}(z)
, and
\tilde{L}/p
is the regular lifting of
L
.
It is clear that all of
M,M^{\prime}
and
M^{\prime\prime}
have the same rank, and that the map
(z,w)\mapsto g(\tau(c)+\tilde{L}(z)+pw)
is rational. So by assumption, if
(g(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}
is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
, then there exists a nontrivial type-I horizontal character
\eta
with
0<\|\eta\|\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
\eta\circ g(\tau(c)+\tilde{L}(z)+pw)\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant for all
z\in\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime}))
and
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. By Lemma
8.1
, we have that
\eta\circ g(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant for all
n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
.
So in the rest of Section
8
, we may make the following assumption.

Assumption 1.

V+c=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},r=0
and
\text{rank}(M)\geq s+13
.

Denote
d^{\prime}=\text{rank}(M)
.
Suppose that we have already proved Theorem
5.1
for all quadratic forms of the form

(8.1)

M_{c,c^{\prime},\lambda}(n_{1},\dots,n_{d}):=cn_{1}^{2}+n^{2}_{2}+\dots+n_{d^{\prime}}^{2}+c^{\prime}n_{d^{\prime}+1}-\lambda

for some
c,c^{\prime},\lambda\in\mathbb{F}_{p},c\neq 0
.
Let
M
be an arbitrary quadratic form of rank
d^{\prime}
. By Lemma
4.3
, there exist
c,c^{\prime},\lambda\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
v\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
c\neq 0
and a
d\times d
invertible matrix
R
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that
M(n)=M_{c,c^{\prime},\lambda}(nR+v)
.

For convenience denote
M^{\prime}:=M_{c,c^{\prime},\lambda}
. Then
R(V(M)+vR^{-1})=V(M^{\prime})
.
Let
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
be rational. By Corollary
3.21
, we may assume that
g
is
pK
-periodic for some
K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Then for any function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
, it follows from Lemma
8.1
(setting
B=V(M)
and
K^{\prime}=K
) that

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M))}F(g(n)\Gamma)=\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M))\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(n)\Gamma)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{z\in\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime}))\cap[pK]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{w\in[K]^{d}}F(g(\tau(-vR^{-1})+\tilde{R^{-1}}(z)+pw)\Gamma)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(z,w)\in\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime\prime}))\cap[pK]^{2d}}F(g(\tau(-vR^{-1})+\tilde{R^{-1}}(z)+pw)\Gamma)\end{split}

where
M^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{2d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is the quadratic form given by

M^{\prime\prime}(z,w):=M^{\prime}(z)
, and
\tilde{R^{-1}}/p
is the regular lifting of
R^{-1}
.
It is clear that all of
M,M^{\prime}
and
M^{\prime\prime}
have the same rank, and that
M^{\prime\prime}
is also of the form (
8.1
) (for the same
d^{\prime}
but with dimension increased from
d
to
2d
). Moreover, the map
(z,w)\mapsto g(\tau(-vR^{-1})+\tilde{R}^{-1}(z)+pw)
is rational. So by assumption, if
(g(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M))}
is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
, then there exists a nontrivial type-I horizontal character
\eta
with
0<\|\eta\|\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
\eta\circ g(\tau(-vR^{-1})+\tilde{R^{-1}}(z)+pw)\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant for all
z\in\iota^{-1}(V(M^{\prime}))
and
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. By Lemma
8.1
, we have that
\eta\circ g(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant for all
n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M))
.
So in the rest of Section
8
, we may make the following assumption.

Assumption 2.

M=M_{c,c^{\prime},\lambda}
is given by (
8.1
).

Let
\psi\colon G\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
denote the Mal’cev coordinate map associated to
\mathcal{X}
.
Now suppose that the concludes holds under the additional assumption that
g(\mathbb{0})=id_{G}
, the
identity element
of
G
.
For the general case, write
g(\mathbb{0})=\{g(\mathbb{0})\}[g(\mathbb{0})]
.
Let
g^{\prime}(n):=\{g(\mathbb{0})\}^{-1}g(n)g(\mathbb{0})^{-1}\{g(\mathbb{0})\}
. Then
g^{\prime}
is rational by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula and
g(n)\Gamma=\{g(\mathbb{0})\}g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Since
\|F(\{g(\mathbb{0})\}\cdot)\|_{\text{Lip}}\leq O(1)\|F\|_{\text{Lip}}
by Lemma A.5 of
[
8
]
, if
(g(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M))}
is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
, then
(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M))}
is not
O(\delta)
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
. Since
g^{\prime}(\mathbb{0})=id_{G}
, by assumption, there exists a nontrivial type-I horizontal character
\eta
with
0<\|\eta\|\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that for all
n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M))
,

\eta\circ g^{\prime}(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant, and thus
\eta\circ g(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant.
So in the rest of Section
8
, we may make the following assumption.

Assumption 3.

g(\mathbb{0})=id_{G}
. As a result, we may further assume that
G=G_{0}=G_{1}
.

Now suppose that the concludes holds under the additional assumption that that
\psi(g(e_{i}))
belongs
[0,1)^{m}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
, where
e_{i}
is the
i
-th standard unit vector in
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
For the general case, Write
g(e_{i})=\{g(e_{i})\}[g(e_{i})]
and let
g^{\prime}(n):=g(n)\prod_{i=1}^{d}[g(e_{i})]^{-n_{i}}
.
Then
\psi(g^{\prime}(e_{i}))\in[0,1)^{m}

and
g^{\prime}
is rational by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula.
Moreover, for any type-I horizontal character
\eta
, since
\eta
vanishes on
\Gamma
,
\eta\circ g^{\prime}
differs from
\eta\circ g
by a constant. This enables us to prove the general case of Theorem
5.1
.
So in the rest of Section
8
, we may make the following assumption.

Assumption 4.

\psi(g(e_{i}))\in[0,1)^{m}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
.

It is helpful to introduce some further convention of notations.
Throughout this section, we assume that
p\gg_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
.
Denote

M_{c}(n_{1},\dots,n_{d})=cn_{1}^{2}+n^{2}_{2}+\dots+n_{d^{\prime}}^{2},

which is the degree two terms of
M
.
and let
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a regular lifting of
M
, and

\tilde{M}_{c}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a homogeneous regular lifting of
M_{c}
(whose existences are guaranteed by Lemma
A.1
).
Then
M(n)=\iota\circ p\tilde{M}\circ\tau(n)
and
M_{c}(n)=\iota\circ p\tilde{M}_{c}\circ\tau(n)
for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
It is clear that
\iota^{-1}(V(M))=V_{p}(\tilde{M})
.

Let
A
be the
d\times d

\mathbb{F}_{p}
-valued matrix associated to
M
and
M_{c}
. Then

\tau(A)
is the
d\times d
integer valued matrix associated to
\tilde{M}
and
\tilde{M}_{c}
. With a slight abuse of notations, we identify the matrix
A
both as the
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-valued matrix and as the
[p]
-valued matrix
\tau(A)
.

Let
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
be a nilmanifold with filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
. Denote
m:=\dim(G)
,
m_{i}:=\dim(G_{i})
,
m_{ab}:=\dim(G)-\dim([G,G])
(the dimension of the Type-1 horizontal torus of
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
),
m_{\text{lin}}:=m-m_{2}
(the
linear degree
of
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
), and
m_{\ast}:=m_{ab}-m_{\text{lin}}
(the
nonlinear degree
of
(G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}}
).

8.2.
The case
s=1

We first prove Theorem
5.1
for the case
s=1
(the case
s=0
is trivial). We make use of the following corollary of Lemma
4.12
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
p
be a prime number. Let
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a quadratic form of
p
-rank
r
. Suppose that
r\geq 3
.
Then for all
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\backslash pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
we have that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{pK}\cdot n\Bigr)=O(p^{-\frac{r-2}{2}}).\end{split}

Proof.

Note that

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{pK}\cdot n\Bigr)=\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[p]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{m\in[K]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{pK}\cdot(n+pm)\Bigr)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[p]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{m\in[K]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{pK}\cdot n\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{m\in[K]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{K}\cdot m\Bigr).\end{split}

Since
\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, it is not hard to see that
\mathbb{E}_{m\in[K]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{K}\cdot m\Bigr)=0
unless
\xi=K\xi^{\prime}
for some
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, in which case

\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi}{pK}\cdot n\Bigr)=\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[p]^{d}}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{p}\cdot n\Bigr)

and thus the conclusion follows from Lemma
4.12
.
∎

Since
s=1
, we have

m_{\ast}=m
and
G/\Gamma=\mathbb{R}^{m}/\mathbb{Z}^{m}=\mathbb{T}^{m}
.
We may write

g(n)=(b_{1}\cdot n,\dots,b_{m}\cdot n)+(v_{1},\dots,v_{m})

for some
K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
b_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}/pK
and
v_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
. Clearly
g
is
pK
-periodic.
If
(g(n)\Gamma)_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}
is not
\delta
-equidistributed, then by Lemma
3.31
, then there exists a vertical frequency
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
with
|\xi|\ll_{m}\delta^{-O_{m}(1)}
such that

(8.2)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}\exp(\xi\cdot g(n))-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{m}}\exp(\xi\cdot x)dm_{\mathbb{T}^{m}}(x)\Bigr|\gg_{m}\delta^{O_{m}(1)}.\end{split}

where
m_{\mathbb{T}^{m}}
is the Haar measure on
\mathbb{T}^{m}
.
So we have that
\xi\neq\mathbb{0}
. Thus

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}\exp(\xi\cdot g(n))|\gg_{m}\delta^{O_{m}(1)}.

Note that
\xi\cdot g(n)=(\xi B)\cdot n+\xi\cdot v
, where
B
is the
\mathbb{Z}/pK
-valued
m\times d
matrix whose
i
-th row is
b_{i}
.
If
\xi B\notin\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, then since
\text{rank}_{p}(M)\geq 3
, Lemma
8.2
implies that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}\exp((\xi B)\cdot n)|=O(p^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}),

which is impossible if
p\gg_{m}\delta^{-O_{m}(1)}
. So we must have that
\xi B\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, which implies that
\xi\circ g\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a constant. This finishes the proof.

8.3.
Construction of a nilsequence in a joining system

We now assume that
s\geq 2
(and thus
d^{\prime}\geq s+13
), and that Theorem
5.1
holds for smaller
s
(and any
m_{\ast}
), and for the same value of
s
and a smaller value of
m_{\ast}
.
By Corollary
3.21
, we may assume that
g
is
pK
-periodic for some
K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.
By Lemma
3.31
, if
(g(n)\Gamma)_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}
is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
, then there exist a vertical frequency
\xi\in\mathbb{Z}^{m_{s}}
with
\|\xi\|\ll_{m}\delta^{-O_{m}(1)}
and a function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
with
\|F\|_{\text{Lip}}\leq 1
and vertical frequency
\xi
such that

(8.3)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(n)\Gamma)-\int_{G/\Gamma}F\,dm_{G/\Gamma}\Bigr|\gg_{m}\delta^{O_{m}(1)},\end{split}

where
m_{G/\Gamma}
is the Haar measure of
G/\Gamma
.
If
\xi=\mathbb{0}
, then we may invoke the method on pages 504–505 of
[
8
]
to reduce the problem to the
s-1
degree case and then use the induction hypothesis to finish the proof. So from now on we assume that
\xi\neq\mathbb{0}
.

Since
F
is of vertical frequency
\xi
, (
8.3
) implies that

(8.4)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(n)\Gamma)|\gg_{m}\delta^{O_{m}(1)}.\end{split}

By taking squares on both sides of (
8.4
), we have that

(8.5)

\begin{split}&\quad\frac{1}{|V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}|^{2}}\sum_{h\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}\sum_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(n+h)\Gamma)\overline{F}(g(n)\Gamma)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(n,h)\in\Box_{p,1}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))\cap[pK]^{2d}}F(g(n+h)\Gamma)\overline{F}(g(n)\Gamma)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{n,m\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(m)\Gamma)\overline{F}(g(n)\Gamma)\gg_{m}\delta^{O_{m}(1)},\end{split}

where we used Lemma
7.2
and the fact that
|\Box_{p,1}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))\cap[p]^{d}|=|V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[p]^{d}|^{2}=p^{2d-2}(1+O(p^{-1/2}))
by Lemma
4.12
and the fact that
\Box_{p,1}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))
is a
p
-periodic set.
Since
d\geq 5
, it follows from Proposition
4.19
that

\mathbb{E}_{h\in[pK]^{d}}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(n+h)\Gamma)\overline{F}(g(n)\Gamma)|\gg_{m}\delta^{O_{m}(1)}.

By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
a subset
J_{1}
of
[pK]^{d}
of cardinality
\gg_{m}\delta^{O_{m}(1)}(pK)^{d}
such that

(8.6)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}\cap[pK]^{d}}F(g(n+h)\Gamma)\overline{F(g(n)\Gamma)}|\gg_{m}\delta^{\mathcal{O}_{m}(1)}\end{split}

for all
h\in J_{1}
.
Recall that
A
is the
d\times d

\mathbb{Z}
-valued matrix associated to
M
. Since
d^{\prime}\geq 3
, by Lemmas
4.12
and
7.2
, passing to a subset if necessary, we may assume that

(8.7)

(hA)\cdot h,h_{1},\dots,h_{d}\notin p\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all }h=(h_{1},\dots,h_{d})\in J_{1}.

Recall that
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is the quadratic form induced by
\tilde{M}
and
V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}=\iota^{-1}(V(M)^{\iota(h)})
. For
h\in J_{1}
, note that

V(M)^{\iota(h)}=V(M)\cap U_{h}
, where

U_{h}:=\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon M(n+\iota(h))=M(n)\}
. Then we may write
U_{h}=V_{h}+u_{h}
for some
u_{h}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, where
V_{h}:=\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon(nA)\cdot\iota(h)=0\}
.
It follows from (
8.6
) that
V_{h}
is a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension 1.
Let
L_{h}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-1}\to V_{h}
be any bijective linear transformation, and let
M_{h}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
M_{h}(z):=M(L_{h}(z)+u_{h})
and let
\tilde{M}_{h}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be the quadratic form induced by
M_{h}
.
Since
(hA)\cdot h\notin p\mathbb{Z}
, we have that

(8.8)

\text{rank}_{p}(\tilde{M}_{h})=\text{rank}(M_{h})=\text{rank}(M|_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}^{\perp_{M}}})=\text{rank}(M)-1\geq(s-1)+13\text{ for all }h\in J_{1}.

Let
\tilde{L}_{h}/p
be the regular lifting of
L_{h}
. Then

(8.9)

(hA)\cdot\tilde{L}_{h}(z)\in p\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all }z\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1},

Since
V(M)^{\iota(h)}=V(M)\cap(V_{h}+u_{h})
, it is not hard to see that

and

.
Since

is

-periodic, by (

) and Lemma

(setting

), the average

(8.10)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{(z,m)\in(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{h})\cap[pK]^{d-1})\times[pK]^{d}}F(g(pm+\tilde{L}_{h}(z)+\tau(u_{h})+h)\Gamma)\overline{F(g(pm+\tilde{L}_{h}(z)+\tau(u_{h}))\Gamma)}|\end{split}

\gg_{m}\delta^{\mathcal{O}_{m}(1)}

for all

.

We decompose

Note that

\begin{split}g_{h}(n):=(\{{g}_{\text{lin}}(h)\}^{-1}{g}(n+h)[{g}_{\text{lin}}(h)]^{-1},{g}(n)),\end{split}

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{(z,m)\in(V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{h})\cap[pK]^{d-1})\times[pK]^{d}}F_{h}({g}_{h}(pm+\tilde{L}_{h}(z)+\tau(u_{h}))\Gamma^{2})|\gg_{m}\delta^{\mathcal{O}_{m}(1)}\end{split}

.

For groups

Note that

.
It is not hard to check that

{g}_{h}

Since

•

Let
\tilde{M}^{\prime}_{h}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{2d-1}\to\mathbb{Z}/p

\tilde{M}^{\prime}_{h}(z,m):=\tilde{M}_{h}(z)
. It is clear from (

, there exist a type-I horizontal character
\overline{\eta}_{h}\colon\overline{G^{\square}}\to\mathbb{R}

By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
J_{2}
of

, we have that

\begin{split}\eta_{0}\circ g^{\square}_{h}(pm+\tilde{L}_{h}(z)+\tau(u_{h}))\equiv a_{h}\mod\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all $h\in J_{2},m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},z\in V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{h})$.}\end{split}

Since

\begin{split}\eta_{0}\circ g^{\square}_{h}(n)\equiv a_{h}\mod\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all $h\in J_{2},n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}$.}\end{split}

Finally, since

for some constant

.

Denote
\eta_{1}\colon G\to\mathbb{R},\eta_{1}(g):=\eta_{0}(g,g)
and
\eta_{2}\colon G_{2}\to\mathbb{R},\eta_{2}(g)=\eta_{0}(g,id_{G})

\eta_{0}(g^{\prime},g)=\eta_{1}(g)+\eta_{2}(g^{\prime}g^{-1})

Similar to Lemma 7.5 of

.

Similar to the approach on pages 12 and 13 of
[

one can compute that (we leave the details to the interested readers)

(8.17)

\begin{split}Q(n+h)-Q(n)+P(n)+\sigma(h)\cdot n-a_{h}\equiv 0\mod\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for all

8.4.
Solving polynomial equations

Our next goal is to solve the polynomial equation (
8.17
). An equation of similar form was obtained in (4.10) of
[
9
]
. However, equation (
8.17
) differs from (4.10) of
[

]
in the sense that (
8.17
) only holds for

instead of for all

. This makes equation (

) significantly more difficult than (4.10) of
[

]
. In fact, the bulk of Sections

).

Since

is rational and is of step at most

, we have that
P
and

are of degree at most
s
and take values in
\mathbb{Q}
.
Replacing
n

) by

respectively, replacing

in (

) by
h_{1}
, and taking the second order differences, we have that

(8.18)

\begin{split}\Delta_{h_{3}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}Q(n)+\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}P(n)\in\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for all
h_{3}\in J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
(n,h_{1},h_{2})\in\Box_{p,2}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{3}})
.
By Proposition
4.19
(and a change of variable), it is not hard to compute that the number of
(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in\Box_{p,3}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))\cap([pK]^{d})^{4}
with
h_{3}\in J_{2}
is
\gg_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}|\Box_{p,3}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))\cap([pK]^{d})^{4}|
.
Since
d^{\prime}\geq s+13\geq 3^{2}+3+3
, it follows from (
8.18
) and Proposition
7.9
16
16

16

If
K
is not divisible by
p
, then we may replace the set

by
(J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d})\cap[p^{2}K]^{d}
to apply Proposition
7.9
.
that

(8.19)

Q=\frac{1}{q}Q_{1}+Q_{0}\text{ and }P=\frac{1}{q}P_{1}+P_{0}

for some

with

, some polynomials

,
and some

(8.20)

for some

,

and symmetric

.
17
17

17

If

and

were

-valued polynomials, then we could apply Corollary

Our next step is to derive better descriptions for the lower degree polynomials

and

by substituting (

For all
h\in J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and

, it follows from (

)
that

Q(n)-Q_{0}(n)\equiv Q(n+h)-Q_{0}(n+h)\equiv P(n)-P_{0}(n)\equiv 0\mod\frac{1}{q}\mathbb{Z}.

So (
8.17
) implies that

(8.21)

\begin{split}t_{h}\cdot n\equiv b_{h}\mod\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all $h\in J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}$ and $n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}$}\end{split}

for some

where

(8.22)

\begin{split}t_{h}:=q(2h\Omega+u+\sigma(h)).\end{split}

Note that the conditions
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}
remain unchanged if we replace
n
by
n+pn^{\prime}
for any
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. So the condition

is also unaffected if we change

. Therefore, we must have that

t_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}/p,b_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}/p
for all

.

Fix
h\in J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
We temporary convert the language to the
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
-setting.
Recall that
M_{h}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is the quadratic form induced by
\tilde{M}_{h}
, and
by (
8.8
)
\text{rank}(M_{h})\geq 3
.

Claim.
There exist
v_{0},\dots,v_{d-1}\in V(M_{h})
such that
v_{1}-v_{0},\dots,v_{d-1}-v_{0}
are linearly independent.

Fix any
v_{0}\in V(M_{h})
(whose existence is given by Lemma
4.12
). Suppose that for some
0\leq k\leq d-2
, we have chosen
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\in V(M_{h})
such that
v_{1}-v_{0},\dots,v_{k}-v_{0}
are linearly independent. If
k\leq d-3
, then
the number of
v\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
v_{1}-v_{0},\dots,v_{k}-v_{0},v-v_{0}
being linearly dependent is at most
p^{k}\leq p^{d-3}
while
|V(M_{h})|=p^{d-2}(1+O(p^{-1/2}))
. If
p\gg_{d}1
, then there exists
v_{k+1}\in V(M_{h})
such that
v_{1}-v_{0},\dots,v_{k+1}-v_{0}
are linearly independent. If
k=d-2
, then let
Q(v)
denote the determinant of the
(d-1)\times(d-1)
matrix with
v_{1}-v_{0},\dots,v_{d-2}-v_{0},v-v_{0}
being the row vectors. Then
Q
is a non-constant degree 1 polynomial in
v
. So
Q
can not be written in the form
Q=Q^{\prime}M_{h}
for some
Q^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
with
\deg(Q^{\prime})\leq\deg(Q)
. By Proposition
6.3
, there exists
v_{d-1}\in V(M_{h})\backslash V(Q)
. So
v_{1}-v_{0},\dots,v_{d-1}-v_{0}
are linearly dependent. This completes the proof of the claim.

Translating the claim back into the
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-setting, we may find
w_{0},\dots,w_{d-1}\in V_{p}(\tilde{M}_{h})
such that
w_{1}-w_{0},\dots,w_{d-1}-w_{0}
are
p
-linearly independent. Then for all
1\leq i\leq d-1
, it follows from (
8.21
) that

t_{h}\cdot(\tilde{L}_{h}(w_{i}-w_{0}))=t_{h}\cdot((\tilde{L}_{h}(w_{i})+u_{h})-(\tilde{L}_{h}(w_{0})+u_{h}))\equiv b_{h}-b_{h}=0\mod\mathbb{Z}.

Since
w_{1}-w_{0},\dots,w_{d-1}-w_{0}
are
p
-linearly independent, by linearity, there exists
q_{h}\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q_{h}
such that

q_{h}t_{h}\cdot\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq i\leq d-1
.
Since
t_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}/p
, we have

(8.23)

t_{h}\cdot\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{i})\in\frac{1}{q_{h}}\mathbb{Z}\cap\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}.

Recall that
h_{1}\notin p\mathbb{Z}
since
h\in J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Since
t_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}/p
,
we may write
t_{h}=c_{h}(hA)+(0,t^{\prime}_{h})
for some
c_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}/p
and
t^{\prime}_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}/p
.
Since
(hA)\cdot\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{i})\in p\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq i\leq d-1
by (
8.9
), it follows from (
8.23
) that
t^{\prime}_{h}B\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}
, where
B
is the
(d-1)\times(d-1)
matrix with whose column vectors are transposes of
\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{1}),\dots,\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{d-1})
with the first coefficient removed.

If
\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{1}),\dots,\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{d-1}),(1,0,\dots,0)
are
p
-linearly dependent, then the fact that
(hA)\cdot\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{i})\in p\mathbb{Z},1\leq i\leq d-1
implies that
(hA)\cdot\tilde{L}_{h}(1,0,\dots,0)=\tau(c)h_{1}\in p\mathbb{Z}
, a contradiction. Therefore
\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{1}),\dots,\tilde{L}_{h}(e_{d-1}),(1,0,\dots,0)
are
p
-linearly independent and thus
\det(B)\notin p\mathbb{Z}
.
So
t^{\prime}_{h}\in\frac{1}{q^{\prime}_{h}}\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}
for some
q^{\prime}_{h}\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q^{\prime}_{h}
. Since
t^{\prime}_{h}\in\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}
, we have that
t^{\prime}_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}
.
Therefore, we have that

(8.24)

\begin{split}t_{h}-c_{h}(hA)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ for some $c_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}/p$ for all $h\in J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}$.}\end{split}

Next we need to take a closer look at (
8.24
).
Since the map
(b,c)\to\eta_{2}([b,c])
is bilinear, the map
c\to\eta_{2}([g(e_{i}),c])
is a homomorphism. So there exists
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{d})\in(\mathbb{R}^{m})^{d}
such that

\eta_{2}([g(e_{i}),x])=\xi_{i}\cdot\psi(x)\mod\mathbb{Z}

for all
1\leq i\leq d
and

.
Similar to the discussion after (7.15) of
[
8
]
, all but the first

coefficients of

are non-zero, and
|\xi_{i}|\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
.
Denote
\gamma_{i}:=\psi(g(e_{i}))
and
\gamma:=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{d})\in(\mathbb{R}^{m})^{d}
.
Since
g
is rational, it is clear that

.

We need the following variation of Lemma 4.3 of

]
to continue the proof of Theorem

Lemma 8.3
.

Let

for all

.
If
N\gg_{\delta,d,m.p}1
,
18
18

18

We caution the readers that
N
is dependent on
p
. However, this will not cause any trouble for the application of this lemma.

then at least one of the following holds:

(1)

such that

;

for all
1\leq i\leq d
.

Proof.

Since

(8.25)

\Bigl\{\gamma_{0}\Bigr\}+\Bigl\{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}h_{i}\Bigr\}-\Bigl\{\gamma_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}h_{i}\Bigr\}

takes at most
2^{m}
different values, passing to a subset of
V
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that (
8.25
) take a same value for all
h\in V
. Absorbing constant terms by
\beta
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that
\gamma_{0}=\mathbb{0}
.

Since

a\{b\}=\{a\}\{b\}+\lfloor a\rfloor b-\lfloor a\rfloor\lfloor b\rfloor

for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

\beta+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\alpha_{i}h_{i}+\xi\cdot\Bigl\{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}h_{i}\Bigr\}\equiv\beta+\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\alpha_{i}+\lfloor\xi\rfloor\cdot\gamma_{i})h_{i}+\{\xi\}\cdot\Bigl\{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}h_{i}\Bigr\}\mod\mathbb{Z}.

So it suffices to prove Lemma
8.3
for the case
\xi\in[0,1)^{m}
.
By Lemma 4.3 of
[
9
]
, either there exists
r\in\mathbb{Z},0<r\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
\|r\xi_{i}\|_{\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}/N
for all
1\leq i\leq d
(where
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m})
), or there exists
\theta\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
with
0<|\theta|\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
\|\theta\cdot\gamma_{i}\|_{\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}}\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}/N
for all
1\leq i\leq d
.

Since
\xi,\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{d}\in\mathbb{Q}^{m}
, by choosing
N
to be sufficiently large depending on
\delta,d,m
and
p
,
we have that
either
r\xi_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
, or
\theta\cdot\gamma_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
.
∎

Denote
\gamma(h):=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\gamma_{j}h_{j}\in\mathbb{Q}^{m}
.
Then the conclusion (ii) in Lemma
8.3
can be reinterpreted as
\theta\cdot\gamma(h)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Let
\{\gamma(h)\}\in[0,1)^{m}

be the coordinate-wise fraction part of
\gamma(h)
.
Then

\sigma(h)=h\Lambda+\xi\ast\{\gamma(h)\},

where
\ast
is the coordinate-wise product of vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
.
So for all
h\in J_{2}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, it follow from (
8.22
) and (
8.24
) that

(8.26)

q(hB+u+\xi\ast\{\gamma(h)\})-c_{h}(hA)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ for some }c_{h}\in\mathbb{Z}/p,

where

B:=2\Omega+\Lambda
.

Proposition 8.4
.

We have that either there exists
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
r\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
r\xi\in(\mathbb{Z}^{m})^{d}
, or there exists
\theta\in\mathbb{Z}^{m_{\text{lin}}}\times\{0\}^{m-m_{\text{lin}}}
with
0<|\theta|\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
\theta\cdot\gamma_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
.

Proof.

The idea of the proof is to apply Lemma
8.3
. Let
N\gg_{\delta,d,m,p}1
to be chosen later.
For
w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, let
V_{w}:=\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon(nA)\cdot w=0\}
and
Z_{w}=J_{2}\cap\iota^{-1}(V_{w})
. Since

\sum_{w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},wA\neq\mathbb{0}}|Z_{w}|\geq p^{d-1}|J_{2}|\gg_{d,m}\delta^{O_{d,m}(1)}p^{d-1}(pK)^{d},

|Z_{w}|\leq(pK)^{d}
,
and

|\{w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},wA\neq\mathbb{0}\}|\geq p^{d}-p^{d-d^{\prime}}\geq p^{d}-p^{d-2},

by the Pigeonhole Principle,
there exists a subset
W
of
\{w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},wA\neq\mathbb{0}\}
of cardinality
\gg_{d,m}\delta^{O_{d,m}(1)}p^{d}
such that
|Z_{w}|\gg_{d,m}\delta^{O_{d,m}(1)}p^{d-1}K^{d}
for all
w\in W
.

For
w\in W
,
w^{\prime}=(w^{\prime}_{1},\dots,w^{\prime}_{d})\in\iota^{-1}(w)
and
h\in Z_{w}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, note that
(hA)\cdot w^{\prime}\equiv\tau((\iota(h)A)\cdot w)=0\mod p\mathbb{Z}
. Taking the dot product of both sides of (
8.26
) with
w^{\prime}
, we have that

(8.27)

hB\cdot qw^{\prime}+u\cdot qw^{\prime}+\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{d}qw^{\prime}_{i}\xi_{i}\Bigr)\cdot\{\gamma(h)\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ for all $w\in W,w^{\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(w)$ and $h\in Z_{w}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}$}.

Since
wA\neq\mathbb{0}
,
V_{w}
is a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension 1. Let
L_{w}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-1}\to V_{w}
be any bijective linear transformation and let
\tilde{L}_{w}/p
be the regular lifting of
L_{w}
. Since all of
B,\xi_{i},\gamma
are rational, there exists
K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that for any
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, the fractional part of the left hand side of (
8.27
) remains unchanged if we replace
h
by
h+pK^{\prime}h^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
8.1
, the map

\Phi\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}^{d}
given by

\Phi(z,m):=\tilde{L}_{w}(z)+pm

mod
pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is a
{K^{\prime}}^{d-1}
-to-1 map between
[pK^{\prime}]^{d-1}\times[K]^{d}
and
\iota^{-1}(V_{w})\cap[pK]^{d}
.
Since
|Z_{w}|=|\iota^{-1}(V_{w})\cap J_{2}|\gg_{d,m}\delta^{O_{d,m}(1)}p^{d-1}K^{d}
, we have that
|\Phi^{-1}(Z_{w})\cap([pK^{\prime}]^{d-1}\times[K]^{d})|\gg_{d,m}\delta^{O_{d,m}(1)}(pK^{\prime})^{d-1}K^{d}
.
So
there exists some
m_{w}\in[K]^{d}
such that the set

I_{w}:=\{z\in[pK^{\prime}]^{d-1}\colon\Phi(z,m_{w})\in Z_{w}+pK\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}

is of cardinality
\gg_{d,m}\delta^{O_{d,m}(1)}(pK^{\prime})^{d-1}
.
So it follows from (
8.27
) that

(8.28)

\tilde{L}_{w}(z)B\cdot qw^{\prime}+u+(pm_{w}B)\cdot qw^{\prime}+\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{d}qw^{\prime}_{i}\xi_{i}\Bigr)\cdot\{\gamma(\tilde{L}_{w}(z)+pm_{w})\}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}

for all
z\in I_{w}
. By the choice of
K^{\prime}
, we have that (
8.28
) also holds for all
z\in I_{w}+pK^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}^{d-1}
. Note that

\begin{split}\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{|(I_{w}+pK^{\prime}\mathbb{Z}^{d-1})\cap\{-N,\dots,N\}^{d-1}|}{(2N+1)^{d-1}}=\frac{|I_{w}|}{(pK^{\prime})^{d-1}}\gg_{d,m}\delta^{O_{d,m}(1)}.\end{split}

Recall that the last
m-m_{\text{lin}}
coefficients of
\xi_{i}
are zero and so the last
m-m_{\text{lin}}
coefficients of
\xi_{i}
and
\gamma_{i}
does not affect the expression in (
8.28
).
If
N\gg_{\delta,d,m,p}1
, then we may apply Lemma
8.3
to (
8.28
) to conclude that either there exists
r_{w}\in\mathbb{Z},0<r_{w}\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
r_{w}q\sum_{i=1}^{d}w^{\prime}_{i}\xi_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
for all
w^{\prime}\in\iota^{-1}(w)
, or there exists
\theta_{w}\in\mathbb{Z}^{m_{\text{lin}}}\times\{0\}^{m-m_{\text{lin}}}
,
0<|\theta_{w}|\ll_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
such that
