f(n)-f^{\prime}(n)\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}/Q)\cap(\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}/p^{d^{\prime}})=\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we have that

\iota\circ pf\circ\tau=\iota\circ pf^{\prime}\circ\tau=F
is a well defined polynomial in
\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime}})
. It is also clear that the degree of
F
is at most
\deg(f)
, and that
F
is homogeneous if
f
is homogeneous.

Part (ii). Assume that

F(n_{1},\dots,n_{d})=\sum_{0\leq a_{1},\dots,a_{d}\leq p-1,a_{i}\in\mathbb{N}}C_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}n^{a_{1}}_{1}\dots n^{a_{d-1}}_{d}

for some
C_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime}}
. Let

f(n_{1},\dots,n_{d}):=\sum_{0\leq a_{1},\dots,a_{d}\leq p-1,a_{i}\in\mathbb{N}}C^{\prime}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}n^{a_{1}}_{1}\dots n^{a_{d-1}}_{d},

where
C^{\prime}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}
is any element in
\{0,\frac{1}{p},\dots,\frac{p-1}{p}\}^{d^{\prime}}
such that
\iota(pC^{\prime}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}})=C_{a_{1},\dots,a_{d}}
. Then it is easy to check that
f
is a regular lifting of
F
. Moreover,
f
is homogeneous if
F
is homogeneous.

Part (iii).
Suppose first that
f^{\prime}
is a lifting of
F
. Note that both
f
and
f^{\prime}
are
p
-periodic by Lemma
2.7
.
Then
\iota\circ pf\circ\tau=F=\iota\circ pf^{\prime}\circ\tau
. So
pf(\tau(n))-pf^{\prime}(\tau(n))\in p\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
. This implies that
f(m)-f^{\prime}(m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
for all
m\in\tau(\mathbb{F}_{p})=\{0,\dots,p-1\}^{d}
. Since
f-f^{\prime}
is
p
-periodic, we have that
f(m)-f^{\prime}(m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Conversely, if
f(m)-f^{\prime}(m)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, then
pf(\tau(n))-pf^{\prime}(\tau(n))\in p\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
and thus
\iota\circ pf^{\prime}\circ\tau(n)=\iota\circ pf\circ\tau(n)=F(n)
.

Part (iv). It is clear that both
f+f^{\prime}
and
pff^{\prime}
have degrees less than
p
.
The conclusion follows from the fact that

\iota\circ pf\circ\tau+\iota\circ pf\circ\tau=\iota\circ p(f+f^{\prime})\circ\tau\text{ and }(\iota\circ pf\circ\tau)\cdot(\iota\circ pf\circ\tau)=\iota\circ p^{2}ff^{\prime}\circ\tau.

Part (v). It is clear that
f^{\prime}\circ pf\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(\mathbb{Z}/p)^{d^{\prime\prime}})
and
F^{\prime}\circ F\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}})
, both of which are of degree less than
p
. Since
\tau\circ\iota\equiv id\mod p\mathbb{Z}
, we have that
pf^{\prime}\equiv pf^{\prime}\circ\tau\circ\iota\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
and thus

\iota\circ pf^{\prime}\circ pf\circ\tau=\iota\circ(pf^{\prime}\circ\tau\circ\iota)\circ pf\circ\tau=(\iota\circ pf^{\prime}\circ\tau)\circ(\iota\circ pf\circ\tau)=F^{\prime}\circ F.

So
f^{\prime}\circ pf
is a lifting of
F^{\prime}\circ F
.

Part (vi). Since
\tau\circ\iota\equiv id\mod p
, we have that

\tau\circ F=\tau\circ(\iota\circ pf\circ\tau)=(\tau\circ\iota)\circ pf\circ\tau\equiv pf\circ\tau\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}.

∎

Appendix B
Properties for
M
-sets

In this appendix, we provide some elementary properties for
M
-sets. We also present some important examples of
M
-sets which will be used in
[
17
,
18
,
19
]
.

We start with the following lemma on the existence of
M
-representations.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form. For any consistent
(M,k)
-family
\mathcal{J}=\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
, the set
\Omega:=V(\mathcal{J})
admits a standard
M
-representation
(F^{\prime}_{1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})
for some
r^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{J}
is independent, then we may further require
r^{\prime}=r
; if
\mathcal{J}
is nice/pure, then we may further require
F^{\prime}_{1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
to be nice/pure.

Proof.

We may assume without loss of generality
that
v_{M}(F_{1}),\dots,v_{M}(F_{r^{\prime}})
is a basis of
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{v_{M}(F_{1}),\dots,v_{M}(F_{r})\}
for some
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
.
Fix
r^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq r
. There exist
t_{1},\dots,t_{r^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that

\begin{split}t_{1}v_{M}(F_{1})+\dots+t_{r^{\prime}}v_{M}(F_{r^{\prime}})-v_{M}(F_{i})=\mathbb{0}.\end{split}

Therefore,

\begin{split}t_{1}F_{1}+\dots+t_{r^{\prime}}F_{r^{\prime}}-F_{i}\equiv 0.\end{split}

So
V(F_{i})\subseteq\cap_{j=1}^{r^{\prime}}V(F_{j})
and thus
\Omega=\cap_{j=1}^{r^{\prime}}V(F_{j}).

\{F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}
is consistent, so is
\{F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon 1\leq i\leq r^{\prime}\}
. Also since
v_{M}(F_{1}),\dots,v_{M}(F_{r^{\prime}})
are linearly independent,
the matrix

\begin{bmatrix}v_{M}(F_{1})\\
\dots\\
v_{M}(F_{r^{\prime}})\end{bmatrix}
is of rank
r^{\prime}
and does not contain a row of the form
(0,\dots,0,a),a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
. We remark that one can choose
r^{\prime}=r
if
\mathcal{J}
is independent.

Let
X=(x_{i,j})_{1\leq i,j\leq r^{\prime}}
be an
r^{\prime}\times r^{\prime}
invertible matrix and denote

F^{\prime}_{i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{r^{\prime}}x_{i,j}F_{j}
for
1\leq i\leq r^{\prime}
. Then clearly
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{v_{M}(F^{\prime}_{i}),1\leq i\leq r^{\prime}\}=\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{v_{M}(F_{i}),1\leq i\leq r\}
and
\Omega=\cap_{j=1}^{r^{\prime}}V(F^{\prime}_{j}).
Since the matrix

\begin{bmatrix}v_{M}(F_{1})\\
\dots\\
v_{M}(F_{r^{\prime}})\end{bmatrix}
is of rank
r^{\prime}
and does not contain a row of the form
(0,\dots,0,a),a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we may choose
X
in a way such that the matrix
\begin{bmatrix}v_{M}(F^{\prime}_{1})\\
\dots\\
v_{M}(F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form which does not contain a row of the form
(0,\dots,0,a),a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
. So
v^{\prime}_{M}(F^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,v^{\prime}_{M}(F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})
are linearly independent and thus
(F^{\prime}_{1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})
is a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{J}
is nice/pure, then all of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}}
are nice/pure. By the knowledge of linear algebra, it is not hard to see that we may choose
X
in a way such that
F^{\prime}_{1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
are also nice/pure. This completes the proof.
∎

We next provide some basic properties for different notions of
M
-sets.

Corollary B.2
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form.

(1)

Any independent
M
-family,
M
-set, or
M
-representation is also a consistent
M
-family,
M
-set, or
M
-representation respectively.

(2)

Non-consistent
M
-sets are empty.
22
22

22

Conversely, by Theorem
C.3
to be proved later in this paper, consistent
M
-sets are non-empty provided that
d
and
p
are sufficiently large.

(3)

Any consistent
M
-set
\Omega
admits a standard
M
-representation (so consistent
M
-sets are the same as independent
M
-sets).

Proof.

Part (i) follows from the definitions. For Part (ii), if
\Omega=\cap_{i=1}^{r}V(F_{i})
for some non-consistent
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
, then 1 is a linear combination of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r}
. So
\Omega\subseteq V(1)=\emptyset
. Part (iii) follows from Lemma
B.1
.
∎

The next proposition provides us some simple ways to construct new
M
-sets from an existing one.

Proposition B.3
.

Let
d,k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
\mathcal{J}=\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\},F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
be an
(M,k)
-family.

(1)

For any subset
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
,
\{F_{i}\colon i\in I\}
is an
(M,k)
-family.

(2)

Let
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
be a subset such that all of
F_{i},i\in I
are independent of the last
d
-variables. Then writing
G_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-1}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-1})
, we have that
\{G_{i}\colon i\in I\}
is an
(M,k-1)
-family.

(3)

For any bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and any
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
,
\{F_{1}(L(\cdot)+v),\dots,F_{r}(L(\cdot)+v)\},F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
is an
(M,k)
-family.

(4)

Let
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
and
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
be such that
F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}}
are independent of
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
and that every non-trival linear combination of
F_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,F_{r}
dependents nontrivially on some of
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
.
Define
G_{i},H_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+k^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
by

\text{$G_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})$ and $H_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}},n_{k+1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})$}.

Then
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}},G_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,G_{r},H_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,H_{r}\}
23
23

23

Here we regard
F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}}
as functions of
n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}
, which acutally depends only on
n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}}
.
is an
(M,k+k^{\prime})
-family.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{J}
is nice, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i)-(iii);
if
\mathcal{J}
is
consistent/independent, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i)-(iv);
if
\mathcal{J}
is pure, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i), (ii), (iv), and so is the set mentioned in Part (iii) when
v=\mathbb{0}
.

Proof.

We only need to show that if
\mathcal{J}
is
consistent/independent, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i)-(iv), since the other parts are easy to verify.
For any
(M,k)
-integral quadratic function
G
, let
G^{\prime\prime}
denote the degree 2 terms of
G
, and
G^{\prime}
denote the degree 1 and 2 terms of
G
.
Then an
(M,k)
-family
\{G_{1},\dots,G_{t}\}
is

•

consistent if and only if for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{t}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
c_{1}G^{\prime}_{1}+\dots+c_{t}G^{\prime}_{t}\equiv 0
implies that
c_{1}G_{1}+\dots+c_{t}G_{t}\equiv 0
;

•

independent if and only if for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{t}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
c_{1}G^{\prime}_{1}+\dots+c_{t}G^{\prime}_{t}\equiv 0
implies that
c_{1}=\dots=c_{t}=0
.

Parts (i) and (ii) are straightforward. We now prove Part (iii).
Suppose first that

\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}(F_{i}(L(\cdot)+v))^{\prime}\equiv 0
for some
c_{1},\dots,c_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Since
(F_{i}(L(\cdot)+v))^{\prime}-F^{\prime}_{i}(L(\cdot)+v)
is a constant, so is
\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}(L(\cdot)+v)
.
Since
L
is bijective, we have that
\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}
and thus
\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}F_{i}
is a constant. If
\mathcal{J}
is consistent, then this implies that
\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}F_{i}\equiv 0
and thus
\sum_{i=1}^{r}c_{i}(F_{i}(L(\cdot)+v))\equiv 0
. If
\mathcal{J}
is independent, then this implies that
c_{1}=\dots=c_{t}=0
.
This proves Part (iii).

For Part (iv), suppose first that

(B.1)

\sum_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}c_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}})+\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}(c^{\prime}_{i}G^{\prime}_{i}+c^{\prime\prime}_{i}H^{\prime}_{i})(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\equiv 0

for some
c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i},c^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Setting
n_{k-k^{\prime}+j}=n_{k+j}
for all
1\leq j\leq k^{\prime}
, we have that

(B.2)

\sum_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}c_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}})+\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}(c^{\prime}_{i}+c^{\prime\prime}_{i})F^{\prime}_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\equiv 0.

If
\mathcal{J}
is consistent, then (
B.2
) implies that
\sum_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}c_{i}(F_{i}-F^{\prime}_{i})+\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}(c^{\prime}_{i}+c^{\prime\prime}_{i})(F_{i}-F^{\prime}_{i})\equiv 0
.
Since
F_{i}-F^{\prime}_{i}=G_{i}-G^{\prime}_{i}=H_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}
, we have that

\sum_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}c_{i}(F_{i}-F^{\prime}_{i})(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}})+\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}(c^{\prime}_{i}(G_{i}-G^{\prime}_{i})+c^{\prime\prime}_{i}(H_{i}-H^{\prime}_{i}))(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\equiv 0.

It then follows from (
B.1
) that

\sum_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}c_{i}F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}})+\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}(c^{\prime}_{i}G_{i}+c^{\prime\prime}_{i}H_{i})(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\equiv 0.

So
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}},G_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,G_{r},H_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,H_{r}\}
is consistent.

If
\mathcal{J}
is independent, then (
B.2
) implies that
c_{1}=\dots=c_{r^{\prime}}=c^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}+1}+c^{\prime\prime}_{r^{\prime}+1}=\dots=c^{\prime}_{r}+c^{\prime\prime}_{r}=0
.
Substituting this back to (
B.1
), we have that

\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}c^{\prime}_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\equiv-\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}c^{\prime}_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}},n_{k+1}\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}).

This implies that
\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}c^{\prime}_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})
is independent of
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
.
If
c^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,c^{\prime}_{r}
are not all zero, by assumption,
\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}c^{\prime}_{i}F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})
and thus
\sum_{i=r^{\prime}+1}^{r}c^{\prime}_{i}F^{\prime}_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})
dependents nontrivially on
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
, a contradiction.
So
c^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}+1}=\dots=c^{\prime}_{r}=0
and thus
c^{\prime\prime}_{r^{\prime}+1}=\dots=c^{\prime\prime}_{r}=0
. So

\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}},G_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,G_{r},H_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,H_{r}\}
is independent.
∎

Finally, we provide some examples of nice
M
-sets which will be used in
[
17
,
18
,
19
]
.

Example B.4
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form
associated with the matrix
A
.
Clearly, we may write

(B.3)

V(M)=V(M^{\prime})+b

for some pure non-degenerate quadratic form
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
of the form
M^{\prime}(n)=(nA)\cdot n+c
for some
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, for and some
b\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

•

The set
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 0 by Convention
9.3
.

•

The set
V(M)\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 1, since
M^{\prime}
is pure.

•

The Gowers set
\Box_{s}(V(M))
is a nice and consistent
M
-set.
To see this, note that
\Box_{s}(V(M))=\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime}))+(b,\mathbb{0},\dots,\mathbb{0})
.
Let
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+1}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+1}
be the bijective
d
-integral linear transformation given by
L(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s}):=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s},n)
.
By Lemma
4.18
,
(h_{1},\dots,h_{s},n)\in L(\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime})))
if and only if
M^{\prime}(n),M^{\prime}(n+h_{i})-M^{\prime}(n),(h_{i}A)\cdot h_{j}=0
for all
1\leq i,j\leq s,i\neq j
. Since all of these functions forms a nice and independent
M
-family, we have that
L(\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime})))
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
(s^{2}+s+2)/2
, and thus so is
\Box_{s}(V(M))
by Proposition
B.3
(iii).

•

The set

\Omega_{1}:=\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{5})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}\colon x_{1}-x_{i},x_{i}\in V(M),2\leq i\leq 5\}

is a nice and consistent
M
-set.
To see this, note that
\Omega_{1}=L(\Omega_{1}^{\prime})+(2b,b,b,b,b)
, where
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}
is given by
L(x_{1},\dots,x_{5})=(x_{5},x_{1},\dots,x_{4})
and
\Omega^{\prime}_{1}
is the set of
(y_{1},\dots,y_{5})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}
such that
M^{\prime}(y_{i})
and
M^{\prime}(y_{i}-y_{5})
are zero for all
1\leq i\leq 4
. Since all of these functions forms a nice and independent
M
-family, we have that
\Omega^{\prime}_{1}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 8, and thus so is
\Omega_{1}
by Proposition
B.3
(iii).

•

Let
s\geq 2
and
denote
\mathbb{w}:=(w_{1},\dots,w_{5})
,
\mathbb{h}^{\prime}:=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})
and
\mathbb{h}:=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})
with
h_{i},w_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
Let
\Omega_{2}
be the set of
(\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6}
such that

(w_{1},\mathbb{h}),(w_{2},\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M))
,
(w_{3},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s}),

(w_{4},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s}(V(M))
,
(w_{5},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Box_{s-1}(V(M))
.
Then
\Omega_{2}=\Omega^{\prime}_{2}+(b,b,b,b,b,\mathbb{0},\dots,\mathbb{0})
, where
\Omega_{2}^{\prime}
is the set of
(\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6}
such that

(w_{1},\mathbb{h}),(w_{2},\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M^{\prime}))
,
(w_{3},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s}),(w_{4},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime}))
,
(w_{5},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Box_{s-1}(V(M^{\prime}))
.
Let
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6}
be the bijective
d
-integral linear transformation given by
L(\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h}):=(\mathbb{h},\mathbb{w})
.
By Lemma
4.18
,
(\mathbb{h},\mathbb{w})\in L(\Omega_{2}^{\prime})
if and only if
M(w_{i}),M(w_{i}+h_{j_{i}})-M(w_{i}),(h_{j}A)\cdot h_{j^{\prime}}
equal to zero for all
1\leq i\leq 5
,
1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq s+1,j\neq j^{\prime}
,
1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq s+1
,
1\leq j_{3}\leq s
,
j_{4}\in\{1,\dots,s-1\}\cup\{s\}
and
1\leq j_{5}\leq s-1
.
Since all of these functions forms a nice and independent
M
-family, we have that
L(\Omega^{\prime}_{2})
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
(s^{2}+11s+12)/2
, and thus so is
\Omega_{2}
by Proposition
B.3
(iii).

•

Let
M
be of the form
M(n)=(nA)\cdot n
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},v_{0},\dots,v_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},r\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
\Omega_{3}\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+1}
be the set of
(x_{0},\dots,x_{k+1})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
such that
M(x_{i}-x_{j})=M(v_{i}-v_{j})
and
M(x_{i})=r
for all
0\leq i\leq j\leq k,i\neq j
.
Note that
(x_{0},\dots,x_{k})\in\Omega_{3}
if and only if

(x_{i}A)\cdot x_{i}-r
and
(x_{i}A)\cdot x_{j}-r+\frac{1}{2}M(v_{i}-v_{j})
are zero for all
0\leq i,j\leq k,i\neq j
.
Since all of these functions forms a nice and independent
M
-family, we have that
\Omega_{3}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
(k+2)(k+1)/2
.

•

Fix some
\Omega_{3}
defined above. Let
\Omega_{4}
denote the set of
(x,\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2k+1}
such that
(x,\mathbb{x}),(x,\mathbb{x}^{\prime})\in\Omega_{4}
, where
x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
\mathbb{x},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
.
Note that
(x_{0},x_{1},\dots,x_{k},x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,

x^{\prime}_{k})
belongs to
\Omega_{4}
if and only if

(x_{0}A)\cdot x_{0}-r
,
(x_{i}A)\cdot x_{i}-r
,
(x^{\prime}_{i}A)\cdot x^{\prime}_{i}-r
,
(x_{0}A)\cdot x_{j}-r+\frac{1}{2}M(v_{0}-v_{j})
,
(x_{0}A)\cdot x^{\prime}_{j}-r+\frac{1}{2}M(v_{0}-v_{j})
,
(x_{i}A)\cdot x_{j}-r+\frac{1}{2}M(v_{i}-v_{j})
,
(x^{\prime}_{i}A)\cdot x^{\prime}_{j}-r+\frac{1}{2}M(v_{i}-v_{j})
are zero for all
1\leq i,j\leq k,i\neq j
.
Since all of these functions forms a nice and independent
M
-family, we have that
\Omega_{4}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
k^{2}+3k+1
.

Appendix C
Fubini’s Theorem for
M
-sets

In this section, we prove a Fubini-type theorem for consistent
M
-sets. To this end, we need to define the projections of
M
-sets. We start with defining the projections of ideals of the polynomial ring.
Let
n_{i}=(n_{i,1},\dots,n_{i,d})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
denote a
d
-dimensional variable for
1\leq i\leq k
. For convenience we denote
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
to be the polynomial ring
\mathbb{F}_{p}[n_{1,1},\dots,n_{1,d},\dots,n_{k,1},\dots,n_{k,d}]
.
Let
J,J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime}
be finitely generated ideals of the polynomial ring
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
and
I\subseteq\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}
.
Suppose that
J=J^{\prime}+J^{\prime\prime}
,
J^{\prime}\cap J^{\prime\prime}=\{0\}
, all the polynomials in
J^{\prime}
are independent of
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
, and all the non-zero polynomials in
J^{\prime\prime}
depend nontrivially on
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
. Then we say that
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
J
and that
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
J
.

Proposition C.1
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p\gg_{d,k}1
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form. Let
\mathcal{J}=\{F_{i}\in\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}
be a consistent
(M,k)
-family and
I\subseteq\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}
. Let
J
be the ideal generated by
\mathcal{J}
. Then the
I
-projection of
J
exists and is unique.

Proof.

Note that for any
r\times r
invertible matrix
(t_{i,j})_{1\leq i,j\leq r}
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
J
is also the ideal generated by
\{F^{\prime}_{i}:=t_{i,1}F_{1}+\dots+t_{i,r}F_{r}\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}
, which is also a consistent
(M,k)
-family. By the knowledge of linear algebra and the expression of
(M,k)
-quadratic functions, we may choose
(t_{i,j})_{1\leq i,j\leq r}
in a way such that for some
0\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
,
F^{\prime}_{1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
are independent of
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
, and
F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{r}
depend nontrivially on
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
. Since
\{F^{\prime}_{i}\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}
is consistent, setting
J^{\prime}
to be the ideal generated by
F^{\prime}_{1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
and
J^{\prime\prime}
to be the ideal generated by
F^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,F^{\prime}_{k}
, we have that
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection of
J
.
This proves the existence of the
I
-projection (note that
J^{\prime}
and
J^{\prime\prime}
dose not contain 1 since
\mathcal{J}
is consistent).

Now suppose that
J
has two
I
-projections
J^{\prime}
and
\tilde{J}^{\prime}
. Then exist some ideal
J^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
such that
J=J^{\prime}+J^{\prime\prime}
,
J^{\prime}\cap J^{\prime\prime}=\{0\}
, and all the non-zero polynomials in
J^{\prime\prime}
depend nontrivially on
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
.

Pick any
f\in\tilde{J}^{\prime}
. We may write
f=f^{\prime}+f^{\prime\prime}
for some
f^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
and
f^{\prime\prime}\in J^{\prime\prime}
. Then
f^{\prime\prime}=f-f^{\prime}
is independent of
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
. By the construction of
J^{\prime\prime}
, we have that
f^{\prime\prime}=0
and thus
f=f^{\prime}
. This means that
\tilde{J}^{\prime}\subseteq J^{\prime}
. By symmetry
J^{\prime}\subseteq\tilde{J}^{\prime}
and so
\tilde{J}^{\prime}=J^{\prime}
. This proves the uniqueness of the
I
-projection.
∎

Let
\mathcal{J}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
be finite subsets of
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
, and
I\subseteq\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}
.
Let
J,J^{\prime}
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
be the ideals generated by
\mathcal{J}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
respectively. If
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection of
J
with
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
being an
I
-decomposition of
J
, then we say that
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
\mathcal{J}
and that
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
\mathcal{J}
.
Note that the
I
-projection
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{J}
is not unique. However, by Proposition
C.1
the ideal generated by
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and the set of zeros
V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
are unique.

If
I
is a subset of
\{1,\dots,k\}
, for convenience we say that
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
J
if
J^{\prime}
is an
\{n_{i}\colon i\in I\}
-projection of
J
. Similarly, we say that
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
J
if
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
\{n_{i}\colon i\in I\}
-decomposition of
J
. Here
J,J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime}
are either ideals or finite subsets of the polynomial ring.

We are now ready to define the projections of
M
-sets.
Let
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
be a consistent
(M,k)
-family and
let
\Omega=V(\mathcal{J})\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
.
Let
I\cup J
be a partition of
\{1,\dots,k\}
(where
I
and
J
are non-empty), and
(\mathcal{J}_{I},\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I})
be an
I
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
.
Let
\Omega_{I}
denote the set of
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|I|}
such that
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=0
for all
f\in\mathcal{J}_{I}
and
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|J|}
.
Note that all
f\in\mathcal{J}_{I}
are independent of
(n_{j})_{j\in J}
, and that
\Omega_{I}
is independent of the choice of the
I
-decomposition.
We say that
\Omega_{I}
is an
I
-projection
of
\Omega
.

For
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|I|}
, let
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
be the set of
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|J|}
such that
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=0
for all
f\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I}
. By construction, for any
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}
, we have that
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
if and only if
(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\Omega
. So for all
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}
,
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
is independent of the choice of the
I
-decomposition.

Example C.2
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated to the matrix
A
. Consider the set
\Box_{s}(V(M))
. By Lemma
4.18
, we may write
\Box_{s}(V(M))=V(\mathcal{J})\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s}]
where

\mathcal{J}:=\{M(n),M(n+h_{i}),(h_{i}A)\cdot h_{j}\colon 1\leq i,j\leq s,i\neq j\}

is a consistent
(M,s+1)
-family of total dimension
\frac{s(s+1)}{2}+1
.
Let

\mathcal{J}^{\prime}:=\{M(n),M(n+h_{i}),(h_{i}A)\cdot h_{j}\colon 1\leq i,j\leq t,i\neq j\}

and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}:=\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
.
It is not hard to compute that
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
is an
\{n,h_{1},\dots,h_{t}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
.
So
\Box_{t}(V(M))
is an
\{n,h_{1},\dots,h_{t}\}
-projection of
\Box_{s}(V(M))
.

We are now ready to state Fubini’s theorem for
M
-sets:

Theorem C.3

(Fubini’s Theorem)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r_{1},\dots,r_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime. Set
r:=r_{1}+\dots+r_{k}
.
Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form with
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
, and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
be a consistent
M
-set admitting a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
.

(1)

We have
|\Omega|=p^{dk-r}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))
;

(2)

If
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k-1
, then

\Omega_{I}
is a consistent
M
-set admitting a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
. Moreover,
for all but at most
(k-k^{\prime})r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}p^{dk^{\prime}+r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}-1-\text{rank}(M)}
many
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
, we have that
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
has a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{k^{\prime}+1},\dots,r_{k})
and that
|\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})|=p^{d(k-k^{\prime})-(r_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))
, where
r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}:=\max_{k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k}r_{i}
.

(3)

If
k\geq 2
, and
I\cup J
is a partition of
\{1,\dots,k\}
(where
I
and
J
are non-empty), then for any function
f\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
with norm bounded by 1, we have that

(C.1)

\mathbb{E}_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\Omega}f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\mathbb{E}_{(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}}\mathbb{E}_{(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})}f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}).

The major difficulty in showing (
C.1
) is that the cardinality of
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
is dependent on the choice of
(n_{i})_{i\in I}
.
However, under a careful computation, one can show that for almost all choices of
(n_{i})_{i\in I}
, the cardinalities of
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
are almost the same. Therefore, it is still possible to write the average of
f
as two iterated averages, at the cost of an error term
O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2})
.

Proof of Theorem
C.3
.

We first assume that
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
and
J=\{k^{\prime}+1,\dots,k\}
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
.
We prove Theorem
C.3
by induction with respect to
k
. We first show that Theorem
C.3
holds if
k=1
. Let
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
be a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
(which exists by Corollary
B.2
(iii)). Since
k=1
, either
F_{1}(n)=(nA)\cdot n+v_{1}\cdot n+u_{1}
and
F_{i}(n)=v_{i}\cdot n+u_{i},2\leq i\leq r
for some linearly independent
v_{2},\dots,v_{r}
, or
F_{i}(n)=v_{i}\cdot n+u_{i},1\leq i\leq r
for some linearly independent
v_{1},\dots,v_{r}
. Since
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
is consistent, in the former case
\Omega
is the intersection of
V(F_{1})
with an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r-1
, and in the later case
\Omega
is an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
. Since
\text{rank}(M)-2(r-1)\geq 3
, by Lemma
4.10
and Corollary
4.14
, we have that
|\Omega|=p^{d-r}(1+O(p^{-1/2}))
in both cases. Since
k=1
, there is nothing to prove in Parts (ii) and (iii).

We now assume that the conclusion holds for
k-1
for some
k\geq 2
and we prove it for
k
.
We may then write
\Omega=V(\mathcal{J})
for some
\mathcal{J}=(F_{i,j}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}\colon 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq r_{i})
, where
F_{i,j}
depends nontrivially on
n_{i}
and is independent of
n_{i^{\prime}}
for all
i^{\prime}>i
.
For convenience denote
\Omega_{k^{\prime}}:=\Omega_{\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}}
and
\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}}):=\Omega_{\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})
.
By Proposition
B.3
(ii), it is not hard to see that
\Omega_{k^{\prime}}=\cap_{1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}}\cap_{1\leq j\leq r_{i}}V(F_{i,j})
(where
F_{i,j}
are viewed as a polynomial in
(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})
since it is independent of
n_{i+1},\dots,n_{k}
) and thus
\Omega_{k^{\prime}}
is a consistent
M
-set admitting a consistent
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
. So by induction hypothesis,

(C.2)

|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|=p^{dk^{\prime}-(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k^{\prime}})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2})).

For convenience also denote

\mathbb{m}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})
and
\mathbb{n}=(n_{k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{k})
.
Note that for all
\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}
, we have that
\mathbb{n}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})
if and only if
F_{i,j}(\mathbb{m},\mathbb{n})=0
for all
i>k^{\prime}
and
1\leq j\leq r_{i}
.

For
1\leq i\leq k
,
since the matrix
\begin{bmatrix}v_{M}(F_{i,1})\\
\dots\\
v_{M}(F_{i,r_{i}})\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form and does not contain a row of the form
(0,\dots,0,a),a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we have that

F_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{i})=(n_{i}A)\cdot(u_{i,j,1}n_{1}+\dots+u_{i,j,i}n_{i})+v_{i,j}\cdot n_{i}+f_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{i-1})

for all
1\leq j\leq r_{i}

for some
u_{i,j}=(u_{i,j,i},\dots,u_{i,j,1})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{i}
,
v_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, and some
(M,i-1)
-integral quadratic function
f_{i,j}
such that the
r_{i}\times(i+1)d
matrix
B_{i}:=\begin{bmatrix}u_{i,1},v_{i,1}\\
\dots\\
u_{i,r_{i}},v_{i,r_{i}}\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form which does not contain zero rows.

Denote
u^{\prime}_{i,j}:=(u_{i,j,i},\dots,u_{i,j,k^{\prime}+1})
and
v^{\prime}_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}}):=v_{i,j}+(u_{i,j,1}n_{1}+\dots+u_{i,j,k^{\prime}}n_{k^{\prime}})A\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
Note that if we view
n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}}
as constants and consider
F_{i,j}
as a function in the varaibles
n_{k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{k}
, then for
k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k
and
1\leq j\leq r_{i}
,

F_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{i})=(n_{i}A)\cdot(u_{i,j,k^{\prime}+1}n_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+u_{i,j,i}n_{i})+v^{\prime}_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})\cdot n_{i}+f_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{i-1})

and thus

v_{M}(F_{i,j}(\mathbb{m},\cdot))=(0,\dots,0,u^{\prime}_{i,j},v^{\prime}_{i,j}(\mathbb{m}),\dots),

where
u^{\prime}_{i,j}
starts from the
n_{i}^{2}
coordinate and
v^{\prime}_{i,j}(\mathbb{m})
terminates right before the
n_{i-1}^{2}
coordinate (and we do not care about the remaining coordinates).

Let
Z_{k^{\prime}}
denote the set of
\mathbb{m}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
such that the
r_{i}\times(i-k^{\prime}+1)d
matrix
B_{i}(\mathbb{m}):=\begin{bmatrix}u^{\prime}_{i,1},v^{\prime}_{i,1}(\mathbb{m})\\
\dots\\
u^{\prime}_{i,r_{i}},v^{\prime}_{i,r_{i}}(\mathbb{m})\end{bmatrix}

is not of rank
r_{i}
for some
k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k
.
Note that the matrix with

v_{M}(F_{k,1}),\dots,v_{M}(F_{k,r_{k}}),\dots,v_{M}(F_{1,1}),\dots,v_{M}(F_{1,r_{1}})

as row vectors is a block upper triangular matrix in the row echelon form of the form

\begin{bmatrix}B_{k}&\dots&\dots&\dots&\dots\\
&B_{k-1}&\dots&\dots&\dots\\
&&\ddots&\dots&\dots\\
&&&B_{1}&\dots\end{bmatrix}

which does not contain a row of the form
(0,\dots,0,a),a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
(note that there is only 1 column to the right of
B_{1}
, which correspondent to the constant coefficients). For all
\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\backslash Z_{k^{\prime}}
,
k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k
and
1\leq j\leq r_{i}
,
the matrix with

\begin{split}v_{M}(F_{k,1}(\mathbb{m},\cdot)),\dots,v_{M}(F_{k,r_{k}}(\mathbb{m},\cdot)),\dots,v_{M}(F_{k^{\prime}+1,1}(\mathbb{m},\cdot)),\dots,v_{M}(F_{k^{\prime}+1,r_{k^{\prime}+1}}(\mathbb{m},\cdot))\end{split}

as row vectors is a block upper triangular matrix of the form

\begin{bmatrix}B_{k}(\mathbb{m})&\dots&\dots&\dots&\dots\\
&B_{k-1}(\mathbb{m})&\dots&\dots&\dots\\
&&\ddots&\dots&\dots\\
&&&B_{k^{\prime}+1}(\mathbb{m})&\dots\end{bmatrix},

where there is only 1 column to the right of
B_{k^{\prime}+1}(\mathbb{m})
. Since
B_{i}(\mathbb{m})
is of rank
r_{i}
,
this matrix does not contain a row of the form
(0,\dots,0,a),a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
. We may then use elementary row operations to transform this matrix into the reduced echelon form which can be written as

\begin{bmatrix}B^{\prime}_{k}(\mathbb{m})&\dots&\dots&\dots&\dots\\
&B^{\prime}_{k-1}(\mathbb{m})&\dots&\dots&\dots\\
&&\ddots&\dots&\dots\\
&&&B^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}+1}(\mathbb{m})&\dots\end{bmatrix}

for some
B^{\prime}_{i}(\mathbb{m})
, which are in the reduced echelon form and of the same size as
B_{i}(\mathbb{m})
. Again this matrix does not contain a row of the form
(0,\dots,0,a),a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
This implies that
\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})
has a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{k^{\prime}+1},\dots,r_{k})
.
So by induction hypothesis,

(C.3)

|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|=p^{d(k-k^{\prime})-(r_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))\text{ for all }\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\backslash Z_{k^{\prime}}.

We now compute the cardinality of
Z_{k^{\prime}}
.
Fix
k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k
. If
B_{i}(\mathbb{m})
is not of rank
r_{i}
, then there exist
c_{1},\dots,c_{r_{i}}
with at least one of them equal to 1 such that

\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}c_{j}u^{\prime}_{i,j}=\mathbb{0}
and

\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}c_{j}(v_{i,j}+(u_{i,j,1}n_{1}+\dots+u_{i,j,k^{\prime}}n_{k^{\prime}})A)=\mathbb{0}.

If
\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}c_{j}u_{i,j,t}=0
for all
1\leq t\leq k^{\prime}
, then we have that
\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}c_{j}v_{i,j}=\mathbb{0}
and this contradicts to the fact that
B_{i}
is of full rank. So at least one of
\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}c_{j}u_{i,j,t},1\leq t\leq k^{\prime}
is nonzero. Therefore, for all
m\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, then number of
n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
m=\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}c_{j}(u_{i,j,1}n_{1}+\dots+u_{i,j,k^{\prime}}n_{k^{\prime}})
is
p^{d(k^{\prime}-1)}
.

On the other hand,
the number of
c_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq j\leq r_{i}
with at least one of them equal to 1 is at most
r_{i}p^{r_{i}-1}
; the number of
m
with
\sum_{j=1}^{r_{i}}c_{j}v_{i,j}+mA=\mathbb{0}
is at most
p^{d-\text{rank}(M)}
. So

(C.4)

|Z_{k^{\prime}}|\leq\sum_{i=k^{\prime}+1}^{k}r_{i}p^{r_{i}-1}\cdot p^{d(k^{\prime}-1)}\cdot p^{d-\text{rank}(M)}\leq(k-k^{\prime})r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}p^{dk^{\prime}+r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}-1-\text{rank}(M)}.

So Part (ii) is proved by combining (
C.2
), (
C.3
) and (
C.4
).

Since

\text{rank}(M)\geq r_{1}+\dots+r_{k}
by (
C.2
), (
C.3
), and (
C.4
), we have

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\backslash Z_{k^{\prime}}}|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|\\
&=|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\backslash Z_{k^{\prime}}|\cdot p^{d(k-k^{\prime})-(r_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))=p^{dk-(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))\end{split}

and

(C.5)

\begin{split}\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in Z_{k^{\prime}}}|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|\leq|Z_{k^{\prime}}|\cdot p^{d(k-k^{\prime})}\leq(k-k^{\prime})r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}p^{dk+r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}-1-\text{rank}(M)}.\end{split}

Since
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
, we have that

|\Omega|=\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}}|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|=p^{dk-(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2})).

This proves Part (i).

Finally, we have that

(C.6)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{m},\mathbb{n})\in\Omega}f(\mathbb{m},\mathbb{n})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{n}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{n}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})}f(\mathbb{m},\mathbb{n})|\\
&=\Bigl|\frac{1}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}}\sum_{\mathbb{n}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})}f(\mathbb{m},\mathbb{n})(\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}{|\Omega|}-\frac{1}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|})\Bigr|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}}\sum_{\mathbb{n}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})}\Bigl|\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}{|\Omega|}-\frac{1}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|}\Bigr|=\frac{1}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}}\Bigl|\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|\cdot|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|}{|\Omega|}-1\Bigr|\\
&=\frac{1}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\backslash Z_{k^{\prime}}}\Bigl|\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|\cdot|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|}{|\Omega|}-1\Bigr|+\frac{1}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}\sum_{\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\cap Z_{k^{\prime}}}\Bigl|\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|\cdot|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|}{|\Omega|}-1\Bigr|.\end{split}

Note that for
\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\backslash Z_{k^{\prime}}
,

\begin{split}&\quad\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|\cdot|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|}{|\Omega|}\\
&=\frac{p^{dk^{\prime}-(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k^{\prime}})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))\cdot p^{d(k-k^{\prime})-(r_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))}{p^{dk-(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))}=1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

For all
\mathbb{m}\in\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\cap Z_{k^{\prime}}
,

\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|\cdot|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{m})|}{|\Omega|}\leq\frac{p^{d(k-k^{\prime})}|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}{|\Omega|}=p^{r_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+r_{k}}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2})).

Moreover,

\frac{|Z_{k^{\prime}}|}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}=O_{k,r}(p^{(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k^{\prime}}+r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})-\text{rank}(M)-1}),\frac{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}\backslash Z_{k^{\prime}}|}{|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}|}=1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}).

So the right hand side of (
C.6
) is at most

O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2})(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))+O_{k,r}(p^{(r+r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})-\text{rank}(M)-1})=1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2})

since
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
. This proves Part (iii).

We now consider the general case.
Let
\mathcal{J}=(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
be a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
. Let
\sigma\colon\{1,\dots,k\}\to\{1,\dots,k\}
be a permutation with
\sigma(\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\})=I
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, and let
L(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}):=(n_{\sigma(1)},\dots,n_{\sigma(k)})
be the
d
-integral linear transformation which moves the variables
n_{i},i\in I
to the front. By Proposition
B.3
(iii),
\{F_{1}\circ L^{-1},\dots,F_{r}\circ L^{-1}\}
is an independent
(M,k)
-family and
L(\Omega)=\cap_{i=1}^{r}V(F_{1}\circ L^{-1})
.
By Corollary
B.2
(iii),
L(\Omega)
admits a standard
M
-representation with some dimension vector
(r^{\prime}_{1},\dots,r^{\prime}_{k})
such that
r^{\prime}_{1}+\dots+r^{\prime}_{k}=r
. We may now apply Theorem
C.3
for the case
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
and
J=\{k^{\prime}+1,\dots,k\}
to the set
L(\Omega)
and complete the proof.
∎

Remark C.4
.

It is worth noting that Proposition
C.3
has many room for improvement. For example:

•

the lower bound of
\text{rank}(M)
could be made into a function of the dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
instead of a function of
r
, which would give us a better lower bound.

•

by a more careful computation, the error term
O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2})
in (
C.1
) can be improved to
O_{k}(p^{-A_{d,k,r}})
for some
A_{d,k,r}>0
with
\lim_{d\to\infty}A_{d,k,r}=\infty
;

•

the estimate for the sum
\sum_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})\in Z_{k^{\prime}}}|\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})|
in (
C.5
) is very coarse. In fact, for most
(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})\in Z_{k^{\prime}}
, the cardinality of
\Omega_{k^{\prime}}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}})
is much smaller than
p^{d(k-k^{\prime})}
. If we estimate this sum more carefully, it is promising to provide a much better lower bound for
\text{rank}(M)
.

However, we will not pursue these improvements in this paper.

Remark C.5
.

An annoying fact is that
each time we apply Theorem
C.3
for a set
\Omega
, we need to first compute the
I
-projection
\Omega_{I}
, and then the set
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
. This computation is not difficult by using the algorithm in the proof of Proposition
C.1
, but is rather redundant. For conciseness, in this paper as well as in
[
18
,
19
]
, we only provide essential details on computations of the sets
\Omega_{I}
and
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
, instead of providing complete computations.

Remark C.6
.

By Proposition
B.3
(i), if
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
is a consistent
M
-set, then for all
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,k\}
,
\Omega_{I}
is also a consistent
M
-set. So one can repeatedly use Theorem
C.3
to write
\mathbb{E}_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\Omega}f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})
as a multiple average in any way we see fit.
For example, by Lemma
4.18
, if
M
is a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
, then we may write

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{4})\in\Box_{4}(V(M))}f(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{4})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(h_{4},h_{2})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2}\colon(h_{2}A)\cdot h_{4}=0}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{2},h_{4}}}\mathbb{E}_{(h_{1},h_{3})\in W(n,h_{2},h_{4})}f(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{4})+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where
W(n,h_{2},h_{4})
is the set of
(h_{1},h_{3})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2}
such that

M(n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{4}h_{4})=0
for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{4}\in\{0,1\}
with
(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3})\neq(0,0)
. It is possible to further simplify the description of the set
W(n,h_{2},h_{4})
and compute its the dimension vectors. However, in many applications we do not have to specify all the sets appearing in the averages explicitly.

As an application of Theorem
C.3
, we have the following estimate for the cardinality of Gowers sets.

Corollary C.7

(Cardinality of Gowers sets)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r,s\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
. If either
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})
or
\text{rank}(M)-2r
is at least
s^{2}+s+3
, then

|\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))|=p^{(s+1)(d-r)-(\frac{s(s+1)}{2}+1)}(1+O_{s}(p^{-1/2})).

Proof.

By Proposition
4.8
, it suffices to consider the case when
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq s^{2}+s+3
.
Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}\to V
be any bijective linear transformation, and let
M^{\prime}(m):=M(\phi(m)+c)
. By Lemma
4.18
,

|\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))|=|\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime}))|
. By Example
9.5
,
\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime}))
admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector

(1,1,2,\dots,s)
.
We may then apply Theorem
C.3
to conclude that
|\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime}))|=p^{(s+1)(d-r)-(\frac{s(s+1)}{2}+1)}(1+O_{s}(p^{-1/2})).

∎

Another application of Theorem
C.3
is to prove Proposition
4.19
. We leave the details to the interested readers.

We conclude this section with some properties on the dimension vectors of
M
-sets.

Proposition C.8
.

Let
d,k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
be a consistent
M
-set. Suppose that
\Omega=\cap_{i=1}^{r}V(F_{i})
for some consistent
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
.
Let
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
.
Suppose that
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
and
p\gg_{k,r}1
.

(1)

The dimension of all independent
M
-representations of
\Omega
equals to
r_{M}(\Omega)
.

(2)

We have that
r_{M}(\Omega)\leq r
, and that
r_{M}(\Omega)=r
if the
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
is independent.

(3)

For all
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
, the set
\Omega^{\prime}:=\cap_{i\in I}V(F_{i})
is a consistent
M
-set such that
r_{M}(\Omega^{\prime})\leq r_{M}(\Omega)
. Moreover, if the
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
is independent, then
r_{M}(\Omega^{\prime})=|I|
.

(4)

For any bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
, we have that
L(\Omega)+v
is a consistent
M
-set and that
r_{M}(L(\Omega)+v)=r_{M}(\Omega)
.

(5)

Assume that
\Omega
admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,

r_{k})
, Then for all
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, the set

\{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+k^{\prime}}\colon(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}),(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}},n_{k+1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\in\Omega\}

admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}},r_{k+1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
.

(6)

Assume that
\Omega
admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,

r_{k})
.
For
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, the
I
-projection
\Omega_{I}
of
\Omega

admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
.

(7)

If
\Omega
is a nice and consistent
M
-set or a pure and consistent
M
-set, then
r_{M}(\Omega)\leq\binom{k+1}{2}
.

Proof.

Part (i) is a consequence of Theorem
C.3
(i).
Part (ii) can be proved using the method similar to the proof of Corollary
B.2
. Parts (iii) and (iv) follow from Proposition
B.3
(i) and (iii).
To prove Part (v), let
(F_{i,j}\colon 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq r_{i})
be a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
. Then by the property of standard
M
-representations,
all of
F_{i,j}\colon k-k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq r_{i}
are independent of
n_{1},\dots,n_{k^{\prime}}
, and
no nontrivial linear combination of
F_{i,j}\colon k-k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq r_{i}
is a constant. The conclusion now follows from Proposition
B.3
(iv).

Part (vi) is a consequence of Theorem
C.3
(i) and (ii).
Part (vii) follows from Lemma
B.1
and the fact that there are in total
\binom{k+1}{2}
quadratic terms of the form
n_{i}n_{j},1\leq i,j\leq k
.
∎

Appendix D
Irreducibility for Nice
M
-sets

We present the proof of Theorem
9.14
in this appendix. We restate this theorem below for convenience.

Theorem D.1

(Nice and consistent
M
-sets are irreducible)
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime, and
\delta>0
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{K}
be a nice and consistent
M
-set. If
d\geq\max\{2r_{M}(\Omega)+1,4K-1\}
and
p\gg_{d,s}\delta^{-O_{d,s}(1)}
, then

(1)

\Omega
is
\delta
-irreducible up to degree
s
;

(2)

\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
is weakly
(\delta,p)
-irreducible up to degree
s
;

(3)

\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
is strongly
(\delta,p)
-irreducible up to degree
s
.

D.1.
Irreducible property for a sample
M
-set

The proof of Theorem
D.1
is very technical. So
we begin with the following special case of Theorem
D.1
to explain the main ideas:

Proposition D.2
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime, and
\delta>0
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form given by
M(n):=n\cdot n
. If
d\gg_{s}1
and
p\gg_{d}\delta^{-O_{d}(1)}
, then
the set
\Box_{3}(V(M))
is
\delta
-irreducible up to degree
s
.

We will only provide a sketch of the proof of Proposition
D.2
to explain the main idea, as the rigorous proof of Theorem
D.1
will be provided in later sections.
Note that
(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in\Box_{3}(V(M))
if and only if
(n,h_{1},h_{2})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
and

M(n+h_{3})=h_{1}\cdot h_{3}=h_{2}\cdot h_{3}=0.

Suppose we have shown that
\Box_{2}(V(M))
is
\delta
-irreducible for all
\delta>0
if
p\gg_{d}\delta^{-O_{d}(1)}
and we wish to show that the same holds from
\Box_{3}(V(M))
.
Let
P\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a polynomial of degree
s
such that
|V(P)\cap\Box_{3}(V(M))|>\delta|\Box_{3}(V(M))|
.

Step 1: a parametrization for the last variable.
We first denote
x:=(n,h_{1},h_{2})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
and express
h_{3}
as a function of
x
.
For convenience denote
h_{i}=(h_{i,1},h_{i,2},h^{\prime}_{i})
for some
h_{i,1},h_{i,2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
h^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-2}
for
i=1,2,3
.
If
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2}):=\begin{vmatrix}h_{1,1}&h_{1,2}\\
h_{2,1}&h_{2,2}\end{vmatrix}\neq 0
, then let
\phi_{x}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-2}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
denote the linear transformation given by

\phi_{x}(h^{\prime}_{3})=\Bigl(\frac{\begin{vmatrix}-h^{\prime}_{1}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}&h_{1,2}\\
-h^{\prime}_{2}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}&h_{2,2}\end{vmatrix}}{\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})},\frac{\begin{vmatrix}h_{1,1}&-h^{\prime}_{1}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}\\
h_{2,1}&-h^{\prime}_{2}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}\end{vmatrix}}{\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})},h^{\prime}_{3}\Bigr).

It is not hard to see that
h_{1}\cdot h_{3}=h_{2}\cdot h_{3}=0
if and only if
h_{3}=\phi_{x}(w)
for some
w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-2}
. Then for all
x\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
with
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})\neq 0
,
(x,\phi_{x}(w))\in\Box_{3}(V(M))\cap V(P)
if and only if
P(x,\phi_{x}(w))=M(n+\phi_{x}(w))=0
.

Note that
M(n+\phi_{x}(\cdot))
is a non-degenerate quadratic form for most of
x\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
. We may write

M(n+\phi_{x}(w_{3},\dots,w_{d}))=\sum_{3\leq i\leq j\leq d}\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})^{-2}G_{1,2,i,j}(x)w_{i}w_{j}

for some polynomials
G_{1,2,i,j}
of degree
O(1)
.

Step 2: a factorization for the target polynomial.
Next we treat
P(x,\cdot)
as a polynomial of
h_{3}
to derive some properties for
P
using the results from Section
6
.
Since
|\Box_{2}(V(M))|=O(p^{2d-4})
and
|\Box_{3}(V(M))|=O(p^{3d-7})
by Corollary
C.7
, the fact
|V(P)\cap\Box_{3}(V(M))|>\delta|\Box_{3}(V(M))|
implies that
|V(P(x,\phi_{x}(\cdot))\cap V(M(n+\phi_{x}(\cdot)))|>\delta p^{d-3}
for many
x\in\Box_{1}(V(M))
. By Lemma
6.1
, we have that
V(M(n+\phi_{x}(\cdot)))\subseteq V(P(x,\phi_{x}(\cdot)))
for many of
x\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
. By Proposition
6.3
and a change of variable, if
G_{1,2,i,j}(x)\neq 0
, then
M(n+\phi_{x}(\cdot))
divides
P(x,\phi_{x}(\cdot))
with respect to the
B_{i,j}
-standard long division algorithm. So we may write

(D.1)

P(x,\phi_{x}(w))=M(n+\phi_{x}(w))R_{x}(w)

for some polynomial
R_{x}
of degree at most
s-2
.
Since every coefficient of
P(x,\phi_{x}(\cdot))
can be written as a polynomial in
x
of degree
O(s)
divided by
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})^{s}
and every coefficient of
M(n+\phi_{x}(w))
can be written as a polynomial in
x
of degree
O(1)
divided by
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})^{2}
, we have that
R_{x}
takes the form (assuming that
s\geq 2
)

R_{x}(w)=R(x,w)/(G_{1,2,i,j}(x)\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2}))^{s}

for some polynomial
R
of degree
O(s)
. By (
D.1
), we have that

(D.2)

(G_{1,2,i,j}(x)\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2}))^{s}P(x,\phi_{x}(w))-M(n+\phi_{x}(w))R(x,w)=0

for many
x\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
for all
w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-2}
.
Note that the left hand side of (
D.2
) can be written as
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{d-2}}C_{i}(x)w^{i}
for some polynomial
C_{i}
. So
C_{i}(x)=0
for many
x\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{d-2}
. By induction hypothesis, we have that
C_{i}\equiv 0
and thus (
D.2
) holds for all
x\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
and
w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-2}
.

Note that if
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})\neq 0
, then

\frac{\begin{vmatrix}-h^{\prime}_{1}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}&h_{1,2}\\
-h^{\prime}_{2}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}&h_{2,2}\end{vmatrix}}{\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})}-h_{3,1}=\frac{\begin{vmatrix}-h_{1}\cdot h_{3}&h_{1,2}\\
-h_{2}\cdot h_{3}&h_{2,2}\end{vmatrix}}{\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})}\text{ and }\frac{\begin{vmatrix}h_{1,1}&-h^{\prime}_{1}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}\\
h_{2,1}&-h^{\prime}_{2}\cdot h^{\prime}_{3}\end{vmatrix}}{\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})}-h_{3,2}=\frac{\begin{vmatrix}h_{1,1}&-h_{1}\cdot h_{3}\\
h_{2,1}&-h_{2}\cdot h_{3}\end{vmatrix}}{\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})}.

So

P(x,\phi_{x}(h^{\prime}_{3}))-P(x,h_{3})
can be written as
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})^{-s}((h_{1}\cdot h_{3})P^{\prime}_{1}(x,h_{3})+(h_{2}\cdot h_{3})P^{\prime}_{2}(x,h_{3}))
for some polynomials
P^{\prime}_{1}
and
P^{\prime}_{2}
of degree
O(s)
, and

M(n+\phi_{x}(h^{\prime}_{3}))-M(h_{3})
can be written as
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})^{-2}((h_{1}\cdot h_{3})M^{\prime}_{1}(x,h_{3})+(h_{2}\cdot h_{3})M^{\prime}_{2}(x,h_{3}))
for some polynomials
P^{\prime}_{1}
and
P^{\prime}_{2}
of degree
O(1)
. By (
D.2
), we have that

(G_{1,2,i,j}(x)\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2}))^{s}P(x,h_{3})=M(n+h_{3})R_{0}(x,h_{3})+(h_{1}\cdot h_{3})R_{1}(x,h_{3})+(h_{2}\cdot h_{3})R_{2}(x,h_{3})

for some polynomials
R_{1},R_{2},R_{3}
of degree
O(s)
for all
x\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
with
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})\neq 0
and for all
h_{3}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
So

(D.3)

(G_{1,2,i,j}(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2}))^{s}P(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})=0

for all
(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in\Box_{3}(V(M))
with
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})\neq 0
. Moreover, it is not hard to see that (
D.3
) also holds if
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})=0
.

Step 3: removing the
(G_{1,2,i,j}(x)\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2}))^{s}
term using a parametrization trick.
It follows from (
D.3
) that for all
(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in\Box_{3}(V(M))
, either
P(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})=0
or
G_{1,2,i,j}(x)\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})=0
for all
3\leq i\leq j\leq d
.
Since
M(n+\phi_{x}(\cdot))
is non-degenerate, at least one of
G_{1,2,i,j}(x)
is nonzero. So we have that for all
(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in\Box_{3}(V(M))
, either
P(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})=0
or
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})=0
for all
3\leq i\leq j\leq d
.
We are close to the conclusion that
\Box_{3}(V(M))\subseteq V(P)
. However, we need some more effort dealing with the
\Delta_{1,2}(h_{1},h_{2})
term.

Denote
\Delta_{i,j}(h_{1},h_{2}):=\begin{vmatrix}h_{1,i}&h_{1,j}\\
h_{2,i}&h_{2,j}\end{vmatrix}
for all
1\leq i<j\leq d
. Using a similar argument as above, we have that for all
(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in\Box_{3}(V(M))
, either
P(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})=0
or
\Delta_{i,j}(h_{1},h_{2})=0
for all
1\leq i<j\leq d
. This is equivalent of saying that
P(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})=0
unless
h_{1}
is parallel to
h_{2}
. To deal with the case when
h_{1}
is parallel to
h_{2}
, we use a parametrization trick.
Fix any such
(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})
and define
f(t):=P(n,h_{1}+tv,h_{2},h_{3})
for all
t\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, where
v\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is any vector not parallel to
h_{2}
.
Then
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is a polynomial of degree at most
s
. Note that
h_{1}+tv,h_{2}
is not parallel to
h_{2}
unless
t=0
. So
f(t)=0
for all
t\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
. By Lemma
4.10
, this implies that
f\equiv 0
and thus
f(0)=P(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})=0
. This completes the proof of Proposition
D.2
.

D.2.
Some preliminary reductions

We start with some reductions before proving Theorem
D.1
.
In the rest of this section,
if
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{K})
is a function depending only on
n_{1},\dots,n_{i}
for some
i\leq K
, then we write
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{i})=f(n_{1},\dots,n_{K})
for convenience.
Throughout we assume that
p\gg_{d,s}\delta^{-O_{d,s}(1)}
.

By Propositions
B.3
(iii),
9.11
and
9.13
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\Omega=V(\mathcal{J})
for some nice and consistent
(M,K)
-family
\mathcal{J}
.

Let
A
be the matrix associated with
M
.
Since
A
is non-degenerate and symmetric, there exists a
d\times d
invertible matrix
B
such that
BAB^{T}
is symmetric, diagonal and non-degenerate. Note that if
F\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{K}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is a nice
(M,K)
-integral quadratic function, then
F^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{K}):=F(n_{1}B,\dots,n_{K}B)
is a nice
(M^{\prime},K)
-integral quadratic function, where
M^{\prime}
is any quadratic form associated with the matrix
BAB^{T}
. Moreover, the set of
(n_{1},\dots,n_{K})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{K}
such that
(n_{1}B,\dots,n_{K}B)\in\Omega
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
r_{M}(\Omega)
. So by Proposition
9.13
, it suffices to prove Theorem
D.1
under the assumption that
A
is diagonal.

By Lemma
B.1
,
\Omega
admits a nice and standard
M
-representation
(F_{i,j},1\leq i\leq K,1\leq j\leq r_{i})
for some
0\leq r_{i}\leq i
with
r_{1}+\dots+r_{i}=r_{M}(\Omega)
. Assume that

(D.4)

F_{i,1}(n_{1},\dots,n_{K})=c_{i}(n_{i}A)\cdot n_{i}+(n_{i}A)\cdot L_{i,1}(n_{1},\dots,n_{i-1})+u_{i,0}

and that

(D.5)

F_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{K})=(n_{i}A)\cdot L_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{i-1})+u_{i,j}

for all
1\leq i\leq K
and
2\leq j\leq r_{i}

for some
c_{i}\in\{0,1\}
, some
u_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
and some
d
-integral linear transformations
L_{i,j}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{i-1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}

given by

L_{i,j}(n_{1},\dots,n_{i-1})=u_{i,j,i-1}n_{i-1}+\dots+u_{i,j,1}n_{1}

for some
u_{i,j,\ell}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
such that the
r_{i}\times i
matrix

\begin{bmatrix}c_{i}&u_{i,1,i-1}&\dots&u_{i,1,1}\\
0&u_{i,2,i-1}&\dots&u_{i,2,1}\\
\dots&\dots&\dots&\dots\\
0&u_{i,r_{i},i-1}&\dots&u_{i,r_{i},1}\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form and is of rank
r_{i}
. In particular,
L_{i,2},\dots,L_{i,r_{i}}
are linearly independent if
c_{i}=1
, and
L_{i,1},\dots,L_{i,r_{i}}
are linearly independent if
c_{i}=0
.
For convenience we say that
(c_{1},\dots,c_{K})\in\{0,1\}^{K}
is the
type
of
\Omega
.

We first prove Part (i) of Theorem
D.1
. For
1\leq k\leq K
, denote
\Omega_{k}:=\cap_{i=1}^{k}\cap_{j=1}^{r_{i}}V(F_{i,j})
.
We prove by induction that Part (i) of Theorem
D.1
holds for
\Omega_{k}
for all
1\leq k\leq K
.

Suppose we have proved that Part (i) of Theorem
D.1
holds for
\Omega_{k-1}
for some
k\geq 1
. We prove that Part (i) of Theorem
D.1
holds for
\Omega_{k}
(when
k=0
, we prove that Part (i) of Theorem
D.1
holds for
\Omega_{1}
without any induction hypothesis).
If
r_{k}=0
, then
\Omega_{k}=\Omega_{k-1}\times\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and so Part (i) of Theorem
D.1
follows from Proposition
9.12
and the induction hypothesis. So we assume that
r_{k}\geq 1
.

For convenience denote
r:=r_{M}(\Omega_{k})=r_{1}+\dots+r_{k}\leq r_{M}(\Omega)
.
Write
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-1})
and
n_{i}=(n_{i,1},\dots,n_{i,d})
for some
n_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
. For
1\leq i\leq k
and
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,d\}
, write
n_{i,I}:=(n_{i,j})_{j\in I}
.
For
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k-1}
, let
\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
denote the set of
n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})=0
for all
0\leq j\leq r_{k}
. It is clear that
(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})\in\Omega_{k}
if and only if
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
and
n_{k}\in\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
.

D.3.
Proof of Part (i) of Theorem
D.1
for the non-degenerate case

We follow the steps similar to Proposition
D.2
. Depending only whether
c_{k}=0
or 1, the proofs are slightly different. We start with the case when
c_{k}=1
. In this case
r_{k}\leq k
.

Step 1: a parametrization for the last variable.

Write

L_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})A=(S_{k,j,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}),\dots,S_{k,j,d}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))

for some polynomials
S_{k,j,1},\dots,S_{k,j,d}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k-1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
of degree at most 1. Since
A
is diagonal, for all
1\leq\ell\leq d
,
S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
is dependent only on
n_{i,\ell},1\leq i\leq k-1
.
Then we may rewrite (
D.5
) as

(D.6)

F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})=\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}n_{k,\ell}S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})+u_{k,j},

for all
2\leq j\leq r_{i}
.

For
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,d\}
with
|I|=r_{k}-1>0
, let
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
denote the determinant of the
(r_{k}-1)\times(r_{k}-1)
matrix

(S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k},\ell\in I}
. If
r_{k}=1
and
I=\emptyset
, then let
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\equiv 1
.
Then

\Delta_{k,I}
is a polynomial from
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
to
\mathbb{F}_{p}
of degree at most
r_{k}-1
depending only on
n_{i,\ell},1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in I
(since
A
is diagonal).
For convenience denote
I^{c}:=\{1,\dots,d\}\backslash I
.
Fix any
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
with
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then
L_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})A,2\leq j\leq r_{k}
are linearly independent. By (
D.6
) and the knowledge of linear algebra,
F_{k,2}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})=\dots=F_{k,r_{k}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})=0
if and only if
for all
\ell^{\prime}\in I
,

(D.7)

n_{k,\ell^{\prime}}=\frac{R_{k,I,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k,I^{c}})}{\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})},

where
R_{k,I,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k,I^{c}})
is the determinant of the
(r_{k}-1)\times(r_{k}-1)
matrix obtained by replacing the column
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
from
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in I}
by
(R_{k,I,j,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k,I^{c}}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
where

(D.8)

R_{k,I,j,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k,I^{c}})=-u_{k,j}-\sum_{\ell\in I^{c}}n_{k,\ell}S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})=-F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})+\sum_{\ell\in I}n_{k,\ell}S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}).

Let
\Delta_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
be the determinant of the
(r_{k}-1)\times(r_{k}-1)
matrix obtained by replacing the column
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
from
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in I}
by the column
(S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
,
and let
Q_{k,I,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
be the determinant of the
(r_{k}-1)\times(r_{k}-1)
matrix obtained by replacing the column
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
from
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in I}
by the column
(-u_{k,j})_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
.
It follows from (
D.8
) that

(D.9)

\begin{split}R_{k,I,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k,I^{c}})=Q_{k,I,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})-\sum_{\ell\in I^{c}}n_{k,\ell}\Delta_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}).\end{split}

For convenience we regard
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
as the set of
n_{k,I^{c}}=(n_{k,\ell})_{\ell\in I^{c}},n_{k,\ell}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, i.e. the coordinates in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
are not labeled by
\{1,\dots,d-r_{k}+1\}
, but by
I^{c}
.
Let
\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, be the map from
n_{k,I^{c}}
to
(m_{\ell})_{1\leq\ell\leq d}
given by
m_{\ell}=n_{k,\ell}
for
\ell\in I^{c}
, and
m_{\ell}
be the quantity
n_{k,\ell}
defined in (
D.7
) for all
\ell\in I
.
Then
\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}
is a polynomial of degree at most 1 of with respect to
n_{k,\ell}
for all
\ell\in I^{c}
. By (
D.4
), we may denote

(D.10)

F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},m):=F_{k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(m))=(\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(m)A)\cdot\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(m)+L_{k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})A\cdot\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(m)+u_{k,1}

for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
with
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
and
m\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
(recall that
c_{k}=1
).
Then for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}

with
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
and
n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,

(D.11)

n_{k}\in\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})

\Leftrightarrow

n_{k}=\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(m)
for some
m\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
with
F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},m)=0
.

Assume that the diagonal of the matrix
A
is
(a_{1},\dots,a_{d})
.
It follows from (
D.7
), (
D.9
), and (
D.10
) that
F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k,I^{c}})
equals to

\begin{split}\sum_{\ell\in I^{c}}a_{\ell}n_{k,\ell}^{2}+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in I}a_{\ell^{\prime}}(\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{-2}\Bigl(Q_{k,I,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})-\sum_{\ell\in I^{c}}n_{k,\ell}\Delta_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\Bigr)^{2}\end{split}

plus a polynomial which is of degree at most 1 in the variable
n_{k,I^{c}}
.
Since
S_{k,j,\ell}
are of degree at most 1, we have that

Q_{k,I,\ell^{\prime}}
and
\Delta_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
are polynomials of degrees at most
r_{k}-1
. Since
S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
is dependent only on
n_{i,\ell}
for
1\leq i\leq k-1
, we have that
\Delta_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
is dependent only on
n_{i,j},1\leq i\leq k-1,j\in(I\cup\{\ell\})\backslash\{\ell^{\prime}\}
. Therefore, for all
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in I^{c}
, the coefficient of the
n_{k,\ell}n_{k,\ell^{\prime}}
term of
F_{I,k,0}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
can be written in the form

(D.12)

\begin{split}\text{$2G_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})(\Delta_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{-2}$ if $\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}$ and $G_{k,I,\ell,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})(\Delta_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{-2}$ if $\ell=\ell^{\prime}$}\end{split}

for some polynomial
G_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
of degree at most
2r_{k}
which depends only on
n_{i,j},1\leq i\leq k-1,j\in I\cup\{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\}
. If
I=\emptyset
, then we denote
G_{k,I,\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\equiv 1
.

Note that
\{\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(m)\colon m\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}\}
is an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r_{k}-1
, by Proposition
4.8
,
F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
is a quadratic form of rank at least
d-2(r_{k}-1)\geq 3
.
Let
A_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}
be the
(d-r_{k}+1)\times(d-r_{k}+1)
matrix associated to
F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
. Let
i_{\ast}
be the smallest element in
I^{c}
. For
i_{1}\in I^{c}
,
let
\phi_{I,i_{1},i_{1}}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
denote the bijective linear transformations given by switching the
i_{1}
-th and
i_{\ast}
-th components of
n_{k,I^{c}}
. For
i_{1},i_{2}\in I^{c},i_{1}<i_{2}
, let
\phi_{I,i_{1},i_{2}}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
denote the bijective linear transformation
which maps
n_{k,I^{c}}=(n_{k,\ell})_{\ell\in I^{c}}
to a vector whose
first component (i.e. the
i_{\ast}
-th component) equals to
\frac{1}{2}(n_{k,i_{1}}+n_{k,i_{2}})
, second component equals to
\frac{1}{2}(n_{k,i_{1}}-n_{k,i_{2}})
,
and the remaining comments equal to
(n_{k,\ell})_{\ell\in(I\cup\{i_{1},i_{2}\})^{c}}
ordered in an arbitrary way.
Let
B_{I,i_{1},i_{2}}
be the
(d-r_{k}+1)\times(d-r_{k}+1)
matrix induced by the bijective linear transformation
\phi_{I,i_{1},i_{2}}
.
This construction is similar the
B_{i,j}
-standard matrix defined in Lemma
6.2
. We have that for all
i_{1},i_{2}\in I^{c}
,

B_{I,i_{1},i_{2}}
is an
(d-r_{k}+1)\times(d-r_{k}+1)
invertible matrix
such that the
(i_{\ast},i_{\ast})
-th entry of
B_{I,i_{1},i_{2}}A_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}B^{T}_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}
equals to the
(i_{1},i_{2})
-th entry of
A_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}
.

It is not hard to see from (
D.12
) that
for all
i_{1},i_{2}\in I^{c},i_{1}\leq i_{2}
,
the coefficient of the
(i_{\ast},i_{\ast})
-entry of
B_{I,i_{1},i_{2}}A_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}B^{T}_{I,i_{1},i_{2}}
can be written as
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})/(\Delta_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{2}
.
Since
f_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
is not constant zero (recall that
F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
is of rank at least 3), we have:

Lemma D.3
.

For every
1\leq k\leq K
, every
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,d\}
with
|I|=r_{k}-1
and every
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k-1}
, there exist
i_{1},i_{2}\in I^{c},i_{1}\leq i_{2}
such that
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
.

Let
Z_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}
denote the set of
(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
such that
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}),G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
.
We need the following estimate.

Lemma D.4
.

If
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}
is not constant zero, then we have that
|{Z}^{c}_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}\cap\Omega_{k}|\leq\delta p^{dk-r}/2
.

Proof.

Let
Z^{\prime}
denote the set of
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
such that either
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})=0
or
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})=0
.
Let
Z
be the set of
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
such that
|\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})|
is not
p^{d-r_{k}}(1+O_{k}(p^{-1/2}))
. By Theorem
C.3
(ii), (setting
k^{\prime}=k-1,r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=r_{k}
and
\text{rank}(M)=d
),
we have that
|Z|=O_{k}(p^{d(k-2)+r_{k}-1})
. Again by Theorem
C.3
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad|{Z}^{c}_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}\cap\Omega_{k}|=\sum_{\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in Z^{\prime}\backslash Z}|\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})|+\sum_{\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in Z^{\prime}\cap Z}|\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})|\\
&\leq|Z^{\prime}|\cdot p^{d-r_{k}}(1+O_{k}(p^{-1/2}))+O_{k}(p^{d(k-1)+r_{k}-1}).\end{split}

Since
d\geq 2r+1
, we have
d(k-1)+r_{k}-1<dk-r
. So it suffices to show that
|Z^{\prime}|\leq\delta p^{d(k-1)-(r-r_{k})}/4
.
If not, then one of
|V(G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}})\cap\Omega_{k-1}|
and
|V(\Delta_{k,I})\cap\Omega_{k-1}|
is larger than
\delta p^{d(k-1)-(r-r_{k})}/8
. Applying Theorem
C.3
to compute the cardinality of
|\Omega_{k-1}|
and using the induction hypothesis that
\Omega_{k-1}
is
\delta^{\prime}
-irreducible for all
\delta^{\prime}>0
if
p\gg_{d,s}{\delta^{\prime}}^{-O_{d,s}(1)}
, we have that either
\Omega_{k-1}\subseteq V(G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}})
or
\Omega_{k-1}\subseteq V(\Delta_{k,I})
.

Note that
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
depends only on
n_{i,\ell},1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in I
, and certainly
\Delta_{k,I}
is not constant 0. So we may pick some
n_{i,\ell}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in I
such that
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
.
Let
\Omega^{\prime}
be the set of
(n_{i,\ell})_{1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in I^{c}}
such that
(n_{i,\ell})_{1\leq i\leq k-1,1\leq\ell\leq d}
belongs to
\Omega_{k-1}
.
Let
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by

M^{\prime}((m_{\ell})_{\ell\in I^{c}}):=M((m^{\prime}_{\ell})_{1\leq\ell\leq d}),

where
m^{\prime}_{\ell}=m_{\ell}
for
\ell\in I^{c}
and
m^{\prime}_{\ell}=0
for
\ell\in I
. Since
A
is invertible and diagonal, we have that
M^{\prime}
is non-degenerate.

For
1\leq i\leq k-1,1\leq j\leq r_{i}
, let
\tilde{F}_{i,j}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1})^{k-1}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the function obtained from
F_{i,j}
(viewed as a function from
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k-1}
to
\mathbb{F}_{p}
where we forget the irrelevant variable
n_{k}
) by fixing the variables
n_{i,\ell}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in I
chosen above.
Let
\Omega^{\prime}
be the set of
(n_{i,\ell})_{1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in I^{c}}
such that
(n_{i,\ell})_{1\leq i\leq k-1,1\leq\ell\leq d}
belongs to
\Omega_{k-1}
.
Then
\Omega^{\prime}=\cap_{i=1}^{k-1}\cap_{j=1}^{r_{i}}V(\tilde{F}_{i,j})
.
By the construction of
F_{i,j}
,
\{F_{i,j}\colon 1\leq i\leq k-1,1\leq j\leq r_{i}\}
is an independent
(M^{\prime},k)
-family. So
\{\tilde{F}_{i,j}\colon 1\leq i\leq k-1,1\leq j\leq r_{i}\}
is an independent
(M^{\prime},k-1)
-family. Therefore
\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1})^{k-1}
is a consistent
M^{\prime}
-set with total co-dimension
r_{1}+\dots+r_{k-1}
, which is the same as
\Omega_{k-1}
.
Since
d-r_{k}+1\geq 2(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k-1})+1
, by Theorem
C.3
,

|\Omega^{\prime}|\geq p^{(d-r_{k}+1)(k-1)-(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k-1})}(1+O_{k}(p^{-1/2}))
. In particular,
\Omega^{\prime}
is non-empty. Therefore,
\Omega_{k-1}
is not a subset of
V(\Delta_{k,I})
.

We may argue similarly for
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}
. Let
J:=I\cup\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
.
Recall that
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
depends only on
n_{i,\ell},1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in J
.
Since
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}
is not constant zero, then we may pick some
n_{i,\ell}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in J
such that
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
.
Let
\Omega^{\prime}
be the set of
(n_{i,\ell})_{1\leq i\leq k-1,\ell\in J^{c}}
such that
(n_{i,\ell})_{1\leq i\leq k-1,1\leq\ell\leq d}
belongs to
\Omega_{k-1}
.
Let
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-|J|}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by

M^{\prime}((m_{\ell})_{\ell\in J^{c}}):=M((m^{\prime}_{\ell})_{1\leq\ell\leq d}),

where
m^{\prime}_{\ell}=m_{\ell}
for
\ell\in J^{c}
and
m^{\prime}_{\ell}=0
for
\ell\in J
.

Similar to the argument in the previous case, we have that
M^{\prime}
is non-degenerate and that

\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-|J|})^{k-1}
is a consistent
M^{\prime}
-set with total co-dimension
r_{1}+\dots+r_{k-1}
. Since
d-|J|\geq d-(r_{k}+1)\geq 2(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k-1})+1
, by Theorem
C.3
,

|\Omega^{\prime}|\geq p^{(d-|J|)(k-1)-(r_{1}+\dots+r_{k-1})}(1+O_{k}(p^{-1/2}))
. In particular,
\Omega^{\prime}
is non-empty. Therefore,
\Omega_{k-1}
is not a subset of
V(G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}})
. We now arrive at a contradiction and we are done. ∎

Step 2: a factorization for the target polynomial.

This step is carried out by the following proposition:

Proposition D.5
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
1\leq k\leq K
,
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,d\}
with
|I|=r_{k}-1
,
i_{1},i_{2}\in\{1,\dots,d\}\backslash I,i_{1}\leq i_{2}
, and
P\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
be a polynomial of degree at most
s
with
|V(P)\cap\Omega_{k}|\geq\delta p^{dk-r}
. Then

(G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{s+2}P(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})=\sum_{j=1}^{r_{k}}F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})Q_{j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})

for some polynomial
Q_{j}
of degree at most
O_{k,s}(1)
for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
and
n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

Proof.

If
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}
is constant zero, then we may set all of
Q_{j}
to be constant zero. So we assume that
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}
is not constant zero.

By Lemma
D.4
, we have that
|V(P)\cap\Omega_{k}\cap Z_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}|\geq\delta p^{dk-r}/2
.
By the Pigeonhole Principle and Theorem
C.3
(ii), it is not hard to see that there exist a subset
U\subseteq\Omega_{k-1}
with
|U|\gg\delta p^{d(k-1)-(r-r_{k})}
and a subset
U(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\subseteq\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
of cardinality
\gg\delta p^{d-r_{k}}
for each
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in U
such that for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in U
and
n_{k}\in U(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
, we have that
P(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})=0
,
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
and
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
.

For simplicity denote
n^{\prime}_{k}:=n_{k,I^{c}}=(n_{k,\ell})_{\ell\in I^{c}}
and set

P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k}):=P(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k}))
. Then for all
n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, by (
D.11
), we have that
(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})\in V(P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot))\cap\Omega_{k}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
if and only if
n_{k}=\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k})
for some
n^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
with
P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})=F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})=0
. Since
U(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
is of cardinality
\gg\delta p^{d-r_{k}}
, we have that
|V(P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot))\cap V(F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot))|\gg\delta p^{d-r_{k}}
.

Recall that
F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
is a non-degenerate quadratic form of rank at least 3.
Since
G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\neq 0
, by Proposition
6.3
,

F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
divides
P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
with respect to the
B_{i_{1},i_{2}}
-standard long division algorithm. Note that every coefficient of
P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
can be written as
R(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})/(\Delta_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{s}
for some polynomial
R
of degree at most
(r_{k}-1)s
, and every coefficient of
f_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\cdot)
can be written as
R(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})/(\Delta_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{2}
for some polynomial
R
of degree at most
2(r_{k}-1)
.
So the
B_{i_{1},i_{2}}
-standard long division algorithm gives us that

P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})=F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})R^{\prime}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})/(G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\Delta_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{s}

for some polynomial
R^{\prime}
of degree at most
O_{k,s}(1)
.
So

(D.13)

(G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{s}P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})-F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})R^{\prime}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})=0

for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in U
and
n^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
.
We may write the left hand side of (
D.13
) as

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d-r_{k}},|m|\leq s}C_{m}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}){n^{\prime}_{k}}^{m}

for some polynomial
C_{m}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})
of degree at most
O_{k,s}(1)
.
Then we have that
C_{m}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})=0
for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in U
and thus
|V(C_{m})\cap\Omega_{k-1}|\gg\delta p^{d(k-1)-(r-r_{k})}
. By induction hypothesis, we have that
\Omega_{k-1}\subseteq V(C_{m})
. So (
D.13
) holds for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
and
n^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
.

Let
(\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k}))_{\ell}
denote the
\ell
-th entry of
\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k})
.
By (
D.7
) and (
D.8
), for all
\ell\in I
,
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})((\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k}))_{\ell}-n_{k,\ell})
equals to
the determinant of the
(r_{k}-1)\times(r_{k}-1)
matrix obtained by replacing the column
(S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
from
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in I}
by the column
(q_{j,\ell})_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
, where

q_{j,\ell}=R_{k,I,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k})-n_{k,\ell}S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})=-F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in I\backslash\{\ell\}}n_{k,\ell^{\prime}}S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})

for all
2\leq j\leq r_{k}
, which, by linearity, equals to the determinant of the
(r_{k}-1)\times(r_{k}-1)
matrix obtained by replacing the column
(S_{k,j,\ell}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
from
(S_{k,j,\ell^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k},\ell^{\prime\prime}\in I}
by the column
(-F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k}))_{2\leq j\leq r_{k}}
.
This means that
\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})((\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k}))_{\ell}-n_{k,\ell})
can be written in the form

(D.14)

\sum_{j=2}^{r_{k}}F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})Q_{j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})

for some polynomial
Q_{j}\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
of degree at most
r_{k}-1
.
Therefore,

\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})^{s}(P(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})-P_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k}))=\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})^{s}(P(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})-P(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},(\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k})))

can be written in the form (
D.14
) for some polynomial
Q_{j}\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
of degree at most
ks
, and

\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})^{2}(F_{k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})-F_{I,k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n^{\prime}_{k}))=\Delta_{k,I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})^{2}(F_{k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})-F_{k,1}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},\phi_{k,I,\mathbb{n}^{\prime}}(n^{\prime}_{k})))

can be writing in the form (
D.14
) for some polynomia
Q_{j}\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
of degree at most
2s
. Since (
D.13
) holds for all
\mathbb{n}^{\prime}\in\Omega_{k-1}
and
n^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r_{k}+1}
, we have that

(G_{k,I,i_{1},i_{2}}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime})\Delta_{I}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime}))^{s+2}P(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})=\sum_{j=1}^{r_{k}}F_{k,j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})Q_{j}(\mathbb{n}^{\prime},n_{k})

for some polynomial
Q_{j}
of degree at most
